# Slop — Behaviour & Architecture Spec

Oct 2, 2026 · @John

## Overview

Slop replaces a team's separate planning tool and issue tracker: it owns planning, the board, shared records and the agent system, and exposes endpoints (MCP and REST) that implementation tools pull from and push to. This document is authoritative for slop's lifecycle, contracts and rules; the sessionator document is authoritative for local command behaviour.

- **Slop** turns any input into a plan, manages globs on the board, tracks PRs, commits, builds, tests and deployments, and produces time reports.
- **Implementation** is done by Claude Code routines for subs and sames, and by sessionator (or any equivalent developer tool) for supers. Sessionator keeps workspace management (tmux, worktrees, branches); agent management moves into slop.
- **Planning has no repository access.** Slop captures intent; the implementation system investigates the code.
- **Hexagonal architecture.** A framework-free `core/` holds the glob domain, rules and prompts. Everything external (MCP, REST, GitHub, CodeBuild, storage, LLM, notifications) is an adapter behind a port. A lint rule forbids the core importing adapters.
- **Writes go through the core; reads can come straight from the store.** Every change, from any entry point, calls the same application services.

**Project profiles (genericity):** everything project-specific is per-board configuration, not code: the list of dev (branch-deploy) environments (each glob gets one automatically, or is given one at creation or pick-up); optional integrations (CodeBuild results, branch deploys, ATF), whose indicators and checks only appear when enabled; sensitive paths; routine prompts; which labels apply. Anything reporting as a GitHub check run works for any project. No project's environment names, integrations or conventions are hard-coded in slop or its public repo; each board's profile and knowledge are entered in slop, and slop's own development runs as a board with a sparse profile.

**Terms: decision, choice, learning.** Three words, kept apart throughout this document.

- **Decision:** only a *decision glob* (ID `s<board>k<n>`): something a person decides, accepting, editing, retargeting or rejecting it. It is not work and has no branch or PR.
- **Choice:** a record of something decided in the work, extracted by slop from text (an implementation record's `## Decisions`, plans, Clarifications and Assumptions attachments, meetings and inbox items). It has no card, is searchable and shown on the globs it came from, and can be *replaced* by a newer choice (never "superseded"). Nobody decides a choice; it records what was.
- **Learning:** proposed guidance on a decision glob, of type `gotcha`, `pattern`, `agent-behaviour` or `fact` (how the system is now after architecture or operations changed; the drafted change replaces the outdated description). Approving one changes the knowledge base or the agent set. There is no `decision` learning type: an agent records a choice in its implementation record's `## Decisions`, and an accepted learning is not recorded as a choice. Existing decision globs of kind `decision` were migrated to `pattern`.
- **Compatibility:** identifiers keep their old names: the `decisions` table and `decision_sources`, `DecisionPipeline`, the job `decisions`, the MCP fields `decisions` and `scopedDecisions`, the REST paths under `/decisions`, the search source type `decision` and the authority tier `decision` all mean choices (or, for `scopedDecisions` and `POST /api/globs/:id/decision`, decision globs) and are not renamed.

## Globs

A glob is one unit of work on a board. Every glob has an **actor** (machine or human) and a **mode** (how the actor does it). Machine work also carries a **fix flag** (is it fixing something broken?) and a **work kind** (R&D or maintenance, for time tracking). The old `category` and `type` fields are kept as aliases for one release (see the model below).

**IDs** are `s<board><type><n>`: `s1t1` task (new non-fix machine work, including R&D; `s1f1` feature IDs exist only on old globs), `s1b1` bug, `s1h1` hotfix, `s1k1` decision glob, which a knowledge-base item is (a learning's glob; see Decision globs and the knowledge page), `s1a1` action glob (see Action globs below). Each board has an atomic counter per letter; KB items are decision globs, numbered by the `k` counter, so `s1k3` is one record, the item and the glob that shows it on the board; creation is a conditional write, so a collision errors instead of overwriting. IDs are immutable: the letter is derived at creation (see **The ID letter rule**), and an existing ID never changes, even if the fix flag, work kind or mode later changes. Hotfixes are deferred; the h type is reserved only.

**Slop types**

- **Sub:** any self-contained change one routine can implement and merge in one PR without a human design decision: bug fixes, UI/UX tweaks, follow-ups to merged work, small features confined to one area, test and doc changes. Intake chooses the type by *when a person is needed*, not by size (see Intake inference), is optimistic, and when in doubt chooses sub. Size alone is never a reason for a same (settled big work stays one glob, with its parallel parts worked as workstreams inside the run; only work that could each ship alone is cut into independent globs, a scope cut), and overlap with an open glob is never a reason for a same (dependencies handle merge order), and neither is touching several areas. The definition tightens through the learned sub size limit, the board's risk areas and intake's examples. The input is the plan. Implementation starts automatically and merges to master without human review if the sub review passes.
- **Same:** standard non-pairing tasks. Planned by loading the glob with context (plan.md, meeting notes, Slack threads, designs). Triggered manually unless the input says otherwise. PRs are merged by the developer once satisfied with the reviews and results. A routine can optionally implement a same (chosen at creation or pick-up).
- **Super:** PO + developer pairing. Every new super waits in Planning (no branch, run or implementer), however it is made (the web form, `create_glob`, the REST create or `sstor --new --super`); `start` is accepted for compatibility and ignored for a super. A developer picks it up to pair (Pick up copies `sstor --glob <id>`, which opens the pairing session on the branch), and it is never started. The developer implements and marks the glob's draft PR ready for review; sessionator maintains the glob's implementation record.

**Actor and mode.** Globs come in two families, told apart by the actor; the actor's modes are the only ones the glob can have.

| Actor | Mode | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Machine | sub | agents alone; merges itself |
| Machine | same | agents build it, a person reviews and merges |
| Machine | super | pairing: a person and a developer |
| Human | decision | a person chooses (a learning to approve, a design choice) |
| Human | action | a person does something, then marks it done or dismisses it |

For machine work the mode is the slop type described above (a sub whose diff exceeds the learned limit is still converted to a same; sub-to-same conversion is always valid). A human glob has no slop type: `type` is null, and a glob cannot move between the families or between decision and action. The mode control on a glob lists only its actor's modes.

**Fix flag.** A machine glob with the fix flag fixes something broken. It is a hint: the orchestrator chooses `reproduce-first` (reproduce, find the root cause, add a regression test) or `straight build` from the investigator's `## Fixing broken behaviour?` answer, whatever the category or flag, and records the choice in the implementation record's `## Decisions`. The flag is inferred from the request and plan when the glob is created and again when its plan changes (the intake model, with a rule-based fallback on words such as "fails", "broken", "error", "regression"), shown as a small marker, and correctable by a person; the inference records its reason, and a person's correction sticks (re-inference never overwrites it). The learning signals that used to read the bug category read it (bug globs are left out of size learning; "a bug naming this glob within 14 days" counts against a merged sub). **A fix glob is never a super**: a super is pairing, not a fix, so intake never flags one, and an explicit fix with a super mode repairs the mode to sub. Every other combination of mode and fix flag is allowed. The matrix is a core invariant.

**Work kind.** R&D or maintenance, for the time report (`get_report`, % R&D vs maintenance). It is inferred silently from the plan at creation and when the plan changes, never asked for on create, and correctable by an admin in the glob's details (an admin's value sticks). Time worked counts under the work kind the glob had at that moment.

**The ID letter rule.** The letter is derived at creation from actor, mode, fix flag and work kind, and never changes afterwards:

| Glob | Letter |
| --- | --- |
| Machine, fix flag | `b` |
| Machine, no fix flag (maintenance or R&D) | `t` (`f` is no longer issued; old `f` IDs still parse) |
| Human decision | `k` |
| Human action | `a` |

**Category and type as aliases.** For one release `create_glob`, `update_glob` and `list_globs` (and the REST create) still accept `category` (`feature` = R&D and is stored as a task with an `s<board>t<n>` ID, `task` = maintenance, `bug` = the fix flag, `decision`/`action` = the human modes) and `type` (the machine mode) and map them onto the model; glob summaries still show both fields, derived from the new ones, and old data was backfilled (feature, task or bug with sub/same/super became machine with that mode, decision became human decision, action became human action; `fix` from bug, `workKind` rnd from feature and maintenance from task). The old fields are then removed.

**Action globs.** An action is something a person does and then marks done or dismisses (a rollout script to run, a setting in another product, an install). It is not code work: no branch, PR, run, sign-off labels or deploys (a prepared action, below, gets machine preparation, and with a branch record a branch and a PR, but still no labels, environments or deploys). Its plan holds Why / Do this (the script path and run command) / Permissions / Check it worked. Lifecycle: open (Planning) to done (who, when and an optional note) or dismissed (a reason), both Signed Off. A relation with `until: done` holds a glob back while the action is open; both done and dismissed meet it. Any board member may act on an action; there is no reopen. **An action waits for its script (s15f175).** A line `script: <repo path>` in the plan (the grammar of a rollout step's script line; a path with `..` or outside the repo is ignored) names the script the action runs. While an open action's script is not on the base branch, or a start relation of the action (until merged, reason "builds its script", set by `create_glob`'s `needs`/`relations` or `add_relation`) is unmet, `complete_action` is refused and left out of `allowedActions`. The check is computed on every read (`script: null | { path, onBase: boolean | null }` on the glob view, null `onBase` when the code host could not be reached, which blocks nothing), cached per board and path until a push to the base branch, which also tells the board about the open actions whose script was missing. A merge of the glob an action waits for only publishes the action: it has no start to release.

**Takes effect by deploying or by doing (s15t238).** The dividing line between a machine glob and an action is how the work takes effect. Work that **takes effect by deploying** (a same, sub or super: the code is the change, shipped through the pipeline with review labels, environments and after-deploy steps) is a machine glob. Work that **takes effect by doing** (someone applies it to the live thing: an AWS account, IAM or a permission set, GitHub or account settings, a tax office; any code is only how it was applied and the record of it) is an action. The revert test decides: work still needed if the change were reverted is its own action. Actor is who finishes: a human glob ends when a person decides or does it. **Machine preparation** is optional work any human glob can have (a decision has investigation and a draft; an action has a prepared script, policy or document). Intake and the New glob form only **suggest** an action (`suggestedAction` with a reason, from the model's `takesEffect: "doing"`); creating never switches category by itself, because an action has no review gate. A shipped change that also needs a one-off command keeps it as a once step (s15t237).

**A prepared action** carries `preparation: {record, rounds}` in its attributes (no column, no migration): `record` is chosen at creation (`create_glob` `prepare: true`, default `branch`, or `record: artifact | attachment`) because the routine cannot change it mid-run. `branch`: scripts or policy files that later work edits (e.g. `infra/iam/*.json`), written on the action's own branch with a draft PR that only a local agent review and the board's required checks gate (no human PR review, no QA/FR/CR labels, no environments, no Arm label, no after-deploy steps, no `review_change`). `artifact`: a truly one-shot script, stored as a `rollout_script` artifact (`put_artifact`, label `action: <slug>`; accepted only on an action created with that record; fetched with `slop get-artifact`), no branch. `attachment`: not code (a document, form or checklist), an attachment. The state is **derived** (`actionState`, never stored): `open` (a plain action, Planning), `waiting` (prepared, Planning, held by a start relation), `preparing` (Doing, a `prepare` run queued or running, the machine's turn), `preparation_failed` (Failed, Retry offered, a person's turn), `ready` (Pr open for a branch, In progress for no PR: the person's turn to run it, with Done and Failed…), `recording` (Done pressed, outcome recorded; the branch merges through the ordinary armed-merge, `squash_merge`, queue and reconcile machinery, and a merge held up by red checks or a conflict shows the usual Fix checks and Fix conflict) and `closed` (Signed Off). `needsHuman` is true for `ready`, `preparation_failed` and a stuck `recording`. A ready action's one human step is `Run: <title>`, whose commands are the first fenced block under the plan's `## Do this`, with the fetch line first (`sstor --glob <id>` for a branch, `slop get-artifact <id> rollout_script --label … --out …` for an artifact). Run purpose `prepare`: a preparation round, and also the Fix checks run on an action's record; it ends `completed` at `mark_ready`, never `watching`, and never runs the script itself (paid-infra and account-changing commands are the person's). The s15f175 rule (an action waits until its script is on the base branch) stays for **plain** actions, which run a script another glob builds; a prepared action's own script runs from its branch or artifact first and merges afterwards. Rows A4 to A10 below.

| From | Who and what | To |
| --- | --- | --- |
| (none) | create an action (`create_glob` with category `action`, no type; that is, actor human, mode action) | Planning |
| Planning | `complete_action` (optional note): records the outcome and releases the globs waiting on it. Refused with `invalid_transition` while a start relation of the action is unmet ("Waits for s14t11 to merge": the glob that builds its script) or while the script its plan names is not on the board's base branch ("script not on main yet: <path>"); `dismiss_action` is never held | Signed Off |
| Planning | `dismiss_action` (a reason is required): the same, recorded as dismissed | Signed Off |
| Signed Off | the same command again (`complete_action` after done, `dismiss_action` after dismissed): nothing changes | Signed Off |
| Signed Off | the opposite command (`complete_action` after a dismissal, `dismiss_action` after done): refused with `invalid_transition` ("This action was dismissed" / "This action is already done") | Signed Off |
| Planning | pick up or take over (`pick_up`, REST `pick-up`, `take-over`): refused with `invalid_transition` ("An action is not picked up: mark it done or dismiss it"); an action has no branch, run or implementer | Planning |
| (none) | **A1 with preparation** (`create_glob` with `prepare: true`, optional `record`): the action is created with `preparation: {record, rounds: 1}`, goes to Doing at once, queues a `prepare` run and, for a branch record, provisions its branch and draft PR (labels none, environment none). Held by an unmet start relation it stays in Planning with `waiting` and starts (A4) when the glob it waits for merges | Implementing, or Planning (waiting) |
| Planning (waiting), Failed | **A4 `prepare_action`** (Prepare, or Retry after a failed round): refused while a start relation is unmet, while a run is live, on a plain action and on a finished one. Rounds +1, `waiting` cleared, a `prepare` run is queued (and the branch provisioned the first time). Retry keeps the same branch and PR: no generation bump | Implementing |
| Implementing | **A5 prepared** (the routine's `mark_ready` with its run ID): a branch record asks the host to mark the PR ready and the ready event moves the action on (a repeat on an already-ready PR takes the "already ready" path); an artifact or attachment record needs the `rollout_script` artifact (`put_artifact`) or an attachment first (refused "Store the script first") and moves at once. The run ends `completed` (nothing watches the checks), no `review_change`, `evaluate_sub_gate` or deploy is queued, `ActionPrepared` is logged. A run that fails (`report_failure`, or a timeout) leaves the action Failed ("preparation failed", Retry offered) | Pr open (branch) or In progress (no PR) |
| Pr open, In progress | **A6 `fail_action`** (Failed…, the output required and masked like a rollout step's): refused after Done, while preparing and on a plain action. Rounds +1; `mergeMode` and `headChecks` cleared; a `prepare` run is queued on the same branch and PR (the PR stays ready; no `reopen_pr`, no generation bump, nothing merged). The masked output (8,000 characters) and the commands (secrets masked) are stored as the attachment `Action run failed (round N)`, which the next round reads as in-scope feedback. `ActionRunFailed` is logged | Implementing |
| Pr open, In progress | **A7 `complete_action` on a prepared action** (Done, optional note; refused in any other state with "not ready to run"): records the outcome (who, when) and releases the globs waiting for it. A branch record starts slop's own merge as Merge does: passed checks go to Merging with `squash_merge`, checks not yet passed arm "merge when checks pass" (`MergeArmed`, `refresh_checks`), and **Done is never refused for red checks or a conflict**: the outcome is recorded and the merge waits for Fix checks or Fix conflict, whose push arms it again. An artifact or attachment record, and a branch whose PR was merged by hand, close at once (Signed Off, `signedOffAt`). The script-on-base rule (s15f175) does not apply to a prepared action: it runs from its branch or artifact first | Merging, Pr open (recording), or Signed Off |
| Merging, Pr open (after Done), any (merged by hand) | **A8 merged** (the merge is observed): after Done the action is Signed Off with no labels, no review, no rollout check and no flagged conflicts: the only effect is `release_waiting`. A merge observed before Done (someone merged the PR) is recorded as `Merged` with no status change, and Done then closes the action without arming | Signed Off (after Done) |
| Any open state | **A9 `dismiss_action` on a prepared action** (a reason is required): a live run ends superseded, a branch record's PR is closed and its branch deleted (`close_pr`, `delete_branch`), and the action is Signed Off dismissed. A prepared action never merges on a dismissal. Not after Done (the record is merging) | Signed Off |
| Pr open, In progress (branch record, recording or ready) | **A10 pick up** (`pick_up`, `sstor --glob <id>`): returns the glob unchanged with its branch so a worktree opens on it, to run the prepared script; no implementer is set and no brief is made. Refused (the text above) for a plain action, while preparing and for an artifact or attachment record | unchanged |

**The revert test** (human steps versus actions) decides where a person's work is recorded: if this glob were reverted, would the work still be needed? No: write a rollout step on this glob (a follow-up of this change that gates its sign-off). Yes, or it is about another repo or board, or it is optional setup: create an action glob instead (`create_glob` with category `action`, no type, on the right board, with a relation if this change needs it first). Never file optional setup as a blocking step. Cross-board relations now exist, so a prerequisite on another board's glob is a relation to that glob, not an action; an action glob remains for work that is not a glob (a script run, a setting, an install).

**Three kinds of human step (s15t246):** a person sees only **Decide** (a decision glob's outcome, or a QA/CR/FR review label), **Answer** (a question within the glob) and **Do** (a command or task: a rollout, a once step, an action glob, any stored work step). Each step kind has a group (`decide`, `answer`, `do` or `housekeeping`) in one table (`HUMAN_STEP_GROUP`). Slop's own next moves (`merge`, `resolve_conflict`, `upload_file`, `unstick`, `pair`, and `done`/`sign_off`) are **housekeeping**: they are the primary action (Merge / Merge and continue, Fix conflict, Upload, Retry or Take over, Pick up) and the corner status, never a step block in the glob view. The step kinds in the domain, MCP and REST are unchanged; only what the glob view lists and labels changes. Inside a work glob there is no separate decision step: a choice is a question, and a review is a label.

**Group** is a plain string attribute, given in the MCP message or inferred by slop from the input. Inference prefers existing group names. Names are normalised for matching; the label colour is derived from a hash of the name. A group is where a broken-up feature lives (see **Groups** below).

**Group-wide decisions (s15f148).** A decision can hold a whole group: `add_relation` / `POST /api/globs/:id/relations` with `holdsBackGroup` writes one ordinary relation per work glob in the group (default gate `start`, until `decided`; globs already past the gate are left out), each marked with the group and a reason naming it, so the decision's `holdsBack` lists them as usual. A glob created in, or moved into, the group while the decision is open gets the relation in the same write; once the decision is decided nothing more is added. Renaming or merging a group carries the mark to its new name. Moving a glob out never touches its relations. A glob that already has a relation to the decision on that gate (even a dismissed one) keeps it as it is, and a hold that matched no glob (all past the gate) leaves nothing for later joiners.

**Groups (s15f147).** A group is only the name on its globs: there is no group row, and a group has no status, branch, run or sign-off of its own. Everything shown about one is derived from the globs that carry the name (work and decision globs; deleted globs aren't there).
- **View.** `GET /api/boards/:b/groups` lists a board's groups (name, `progress` as merged of total, `open`, `counts` by status, `lastActivityAt`; open ones first) and `GET /api/boards/:b/groups/:name` returns one: its work globs (status, type, whose move it is, what holds each), its relations (every active relation of its globs, holding ones first; `crosses` marks one to a work glob outside the group, so a move between groups, which never touches relations, still shows), and its open decisions (those holding a glob of the group, and the group's own decision globs that are still open, each with the globs of the group it holds back). MCP `get_group(board, name)` returns the same view. Names match ignoring case and separators, and a group shows under its most used spelling; a name nobody carries is `not_found`.
- **Progress** is "merged of total": a work glob has merged when it is in Reviewing or signed off; decisions don't count, and a deleted glob is left out.
- **Rename and merge.** `POST /api/boards/:b/groups/:name/rename {to}` writes the new name on every glob in the group, and `POST .../merge {into}` moves the group into an existing one, which keeps its spelling. Each glob changes through `update`, so each gets one `FieldsChanged` event with its own version check (a lost race is retried once; globs that still fail are listed in `failed` and a second call moves them). A name another group already has is a merge, not a rename (`invalid_input`); `backfill` is reserved, a name is at most 60 characters, and anyone who can edit the globs may rename or merge (the same rule as `update`). Relations are never touched.

**Relations and Start after (s15f127).** One glob can be held back by another through a **relation**: a row in the `relations` table (`id`, `boardId`, `from`, `to`, `gate`, `until`, `reason`, `evidence`, `source`, `dismissedAt`, `dismissedPlanHash`, `overriddenAt`, `createdAt`; one per `from`, `to` and gate; no foreign key on `to`, so a deleted glob leaves the row). `gate` is what `from` can't do yet (`start`, `merge`, `sign_off`) and `until` what `to` must reach (`merged`, `decided` for a decision glob's accept, reject or closed outcome, `done` for an action glob that was completed or dismissed, `signed_off`); `source` is `person`, `inferred`, `learned`, `agent`, `mention` (added by slop from a sentence that names the other glob, see Automatic relations; `auto` on rows written before s15t250) or `split` (old rows only: a split no longer creates relations of its own, see Splitting a glob). Each gate guards one transition and no other: a **start** relation blocks Start, pick up (a warning), the board's Start and the run's last check (rows 5, 32 to 34); a **merge** relation blocks the Merge button, Merge and continue, a retried merge and the merge itself (rows 14, 14a, 14b); a **sign-off** relation blocks the move to Signed off, including a decision's (rows 21a, 21b). Every gate can be forced through by a person, each with its own action that overrides only that gate: Start now (row 34) marks the inferred, learned and mention start relations overridden, **Merge anyway** (row 14d) every unmet merge relation and **Sign off anyway** (row 21c) every unmet sign-off relation, whatever its source; none of them skips anything else (checks, conflicts, open questions, labels, rollout steps), and each logs who did it and why. **Start after** is the shorthand for a start relation with the default `until` of what it points at: merged for work, decided for a decision, done for an action (`defaultUntil`), so a glob that needs an action waits until the action is done or dismissed; a list entry for an action that is done already is not recorded. A split part inherits the original's waits with the same default. A decision is never the glob held back: it has no start, merge or sign-off gate. An action is held back only at its start gate (a start relation to any glob of the board holds its Done, see Action globs). The list is set at creation (`create_glob`'s `needs`/`after`, the New glob form's "Start after", `slop new --after`, `sstor --new --after`) and editable in the glob view while the glob has no branch (`update_glob`'s `needs`/`after`); it replaces the relations a person, an agent or an old split set and leaves inferred and learned ones. IDs must exist on the board, can't be the glob itself, and can't make a cycle. The cycle check is over (glob, milestone) pairs (start, merged, signed off), so a glob that signs off after another has merged, while that one starts after the first has merged, is no cycle; it runs under a per-board lock, in the same transaction as the write. IDs that have already merged are ignored (dropped when set), at most 20 per glob and gate. What the glob still waits for is derived from the live state of the other globs, not stored: a relation is *met* once `to` has reached `until` (Reviewing or Signed off for `merged`, unless open rollout steps hold back its dependants; a super that merged with Merge and continue is still open), when it was dismissed or overridden, or when `to` was **deleted**: a deleted `to` releases the gate, the row stays, the dependant's log says "`<id>` was deleted" (event `RelationReleased`) and a glob held only by it starts. One that went back to Planning still holds. Every relation write is one transaction that logs an event on `from` (`RelationAdded`, `RelationDismissed`, `RelationReleased`) and moves its version, so live views and stale forms see it. Stored on the glob itself: `impliedAfter` (the overlaps the board's merge policy predicted, internal: `{ id, paths, overridden? }`, see Knowledge), `waiting` (`{ since }` while the glob is held in Planning) and `clash` (a warning, see Merging); the old `after` and needs entries of `impliedAfter` are copied into relations by migration 0067 and no longer read. **What holds a glob back is computed on the server** and sent as `holding` on the board list and the glob read: every unmet relation of the glob (any gate) as `{ relationId, to, toTitle, toIsDecision, gate, until, state, reason, source }`, from one read of the board's relations plus one read of the globs they point at that the list does not carry (the list holds only the latest signed-off globs, so a web-side walk would read an old merged `to` as deleted and show a false hold). A single glob read and `get_glob` also carry `relations`: `holding` (the same), `holdsBack` (the relations of other globs that wait for this one and are unmet) and `done` (relations that no longer hold, each with how: `met`, `deleted`, `dismissed` or `overridden`); `after` (what a person, an agent or an old split set) and `waitingFor` and `waitedOnBy` stay. There is no `edges` and no `blockedBy` any more. **Whose move** reads `holding`: a start relation holds a glob in Planning ("waiting on s15f82", "waiting on decision s15k190"), a merge relation a PR that is open, not conflicting and not failing ("merge waits on s15f82", and the Merge step is not due), a sign-off relation a merged glob whose labels are all approved ("sign-off waits on s15f82"), with `waitingOn` the IDs; until then the reviewer's turn and a conflict or failed checks come first. The card says it in its one corner status, built by the web's `holdLine` from `holding` and core's `appliedHolding` (the rule above, exported from `whose-move.ts`): "waiting · s15f145" or "waiting · decision s15k123" for a start gate, "merge held · decision s15k123" for a merge gate, "sign-off held · s15f145" for a sign-off gate, the first hold plus "+N" for the others; the tooltip lists every hold with where it stands and its reason, a click opens the glob's Related section, and a decision (a person must decide) or an action held for its script (a person will do it once it is free) has the human icon, any other hold the hourglass. The card also shows "clashes with s15t7" while `clash` is set; the glob view lists what it waits for and what waits for it, offers the `after` field while the glob has no branch, and Start now (the board also offers it as a move from Planning). A merge held for a merge relation shows as a waiting machine step (`merge_held`): it is waiting, not stuck, so the card, the stuck hint and the reconcile job leave it alone. **Reasons, rollout and `ignoredAfter` (s15b148).** An agent relation (and `holds`, `supersedes`) requires a one-line reason (at most 300 characters; refused otherwise, and the message names both ways to pass it: `reason` next to `needs`/`after`, or `relations: [{ to, reason }]`). `until: merged` means merged and rolled out: a relation to a glob that has merged but still has due rollout steps is KEPT and keeps holding, and is dropped (or met) only once those steps are done or dismissed (`rolled_out` is accepted as an input alias). The IDs of `needs`/`after` that were ignored (already merged and rolled out, deleted, unknown) come back as `ignoredAfter` so the agent can fix its plan. Replies of `create_glob`/`update_glob` carry `after`, `waitingFor`, `ignoredAfter`, `holds` and `typeNote`.

**Cross-board relations (s15b148).** A relation's `to` may be a glob on another board. It is stored once, on the holder's board row (`boardId` is the holder's board), and shown on both boards (the held glob's holding list, the other glob's "holds back"). Permission: edit on the holder plus membership of the target's board; a viewer without access to the target's board sees it redacted as "a glob on another board" (no id, title or status), while the hold itself still shows. One instance-wide relations lock serialises every relation write (create, update, add, split, the automatic pass, dependency learning), not only cross-board ones. The cycle check runs in the same transaction over the glob's board plus the forward closure of the new edge's targets across boards (capped at 1000 extra rows; beyond that the write is refused as too large to check), and every relation writer uses it, including the inferred and learned ones. Text built from a relation (refusals, the `Waiting` note, event data, run failure reasons) names a glob on another board only as "a glob on another board". A deleted or unreachable target releases the gate as for a deleted `to`.

**Supersedes (s15b148).** A glob can replace another: `supersedes` on `create_glob`/`update_glob`, or `add_relation` with `supersedes`, writes a row on the superseded glob (a reason is required; `update_glob` takes `supersedes`, `add_relation` writes it with `supersedes`). Effects: every open step of the superseded glob (any kind, not only rollout steps, and also those added later) is dismissed with the reason "superseded by A", and its card shows the label "superseded by A". No relation is touched. Deleting the superseder removes the label. It is never automatic: slop only leaves a card suggestion when a plan or record says it replaces a glob.

**Reverse relations: `holds` (s15b148).** `holds` (alias `follows_up`) on `create_glob` (not on `update_glob`: use `add_relation` with `holdsBack` there) is a relation written the other way round: the new glob holds back existing globs (any board) at a gate, default `sign_off`, with a required reason. It is for follow-ups and fixes (a fix for a failed rollout, a continuation of a merged glob, a slop-side change a rollout needs): the held glob cannot sign off until the new glob has merged and rolled out. Permission as for cross-board relations. The reply lists it under `holds`.

**Automatic relations (s15b148, changed in s15t250).** Slop adds a relation by itself for every glob that the plan, the implementation record, the rollout steps or the attachments mention, unless the sentence says otherwise, and never asks about it: there is no Suggestions block. The relation has `source: mention` (`auto` on older rows; shown "from a mention", with the sentence as the chip's hover), one-click Dismiss through `dismiss_relation`, and a dismissed one is remembered by the pair so it is not added again. Triggers: a plan save, a record save, a step parse and an attachment add. A prefilter looks for glob ids in the changed text only; with none, no model is called. Scans are debounced 30 s per glob, cached by (glob, id, sentence hash) and sent in one batched call to the cheap model. One relation per mentioned glob, however many sentences name it: the reason is the first mentioning sentence (the plan before the record, attachments and steps) as plain text (links, code and emphasis marks removed) cut at 300 characters. The gate: a prerequisite the model is sure of keeps the gate it chose (`start`, `merge` or `sign_off`, a `start` gate on a glob that has already started becomes `merge`); a mention the model is unsure of (or cannot name a gate for) gets `start` while the glob is in Planning and `merge` after. `until` is the target's default (`merged`, `decided` for a decision, `done` for an action). A mention is skipped (and remembered as context, so it is not asked again) when the target is merged and rolled out, deleted or not viewable, already related in either direction (any gate, dismissed too), would close a cycle or is past the gate; a guess (unsure) about a glob on another board is skipped, a confident prerequisite there is added. "Fixes sXtY" and "continues sXtY" put the relation on the other glob as before; "replaces sXtY", "supersedes sXtY" and "undoes sXtY" add nothing (superseding dismisses the other glob's steps, so it stays a person's choice). The log says where it came from: `RelationAdded` carries `from` (plan, implementation record, attachment or step) and a note, "added relation to s15t246 from a mention in the plan". The only question left on the card is a failed step's "is there a fix glob?" (under "Failed step"; see A failed rollout step). The cost shows in the board settings as ledger source `relation_scan`. Migrations 0081 and 0082 add the supersedes kind and the scan cache; 0083 deletes the open mention rows the old Suggestions block showed and queues one `scan_relations` job per affected glob, so their mentions become relations (or context rows). Agents must still pass relations explicitly: the scan is a backstop and never replaces them.

**Relation learning (s15t251).** Slop measures how often each source's relations are right on each board and lets that decide how readily it creates gating relations. Every cause is a row in `relation_signals` (`boardId`, `fromGlob`, `toGlob`, `relationId` or null, `source`, `signal`, `at`, `evidence`, unique `key` so a cause counts once; a relation has one outcome key, so it counts once toward precision whichever of override, dismissal, promotion, check verdict or failed run comes first and a later one is ignored): false positives `fp_override` (Start, Merge or Sign off anyway), `fp_dismissed` (dismissed before it was met) and `fp_not_needed` (the post-merge check); false negatives `fn_person_added` (a person added a relation the glob had no row for; a group hold is not counted), `fn_conflict` and `fn_red_main` (two globs without a relation collided, source `none`; the red-main check reads at most 20 candidate globs, outside any transaction, and runs off the base-check write) and `fn_failed_run` (a run failed for code that was not merged; the learned relation is the miss); true positives `tp_needed` (the check or a failed run says the relation was needed) and `tp_promoted`. Precision and recall are computed on read over a rolling window (default 90 days): precision = needed / (needed + not needed) per source; recall only for `inferred` and `agent`, where every false negative counts against both. `auto` rows count as `mention`. Posture (`relation_learning.settings` holds the admin's pins, `{ windowDays, precisionThreshold, recallThreshold, minSamples, postures, history }`, validated in core): only `mention` and `inferred` can be **weak**: below the precision threshold (default 0.5) over at least the minimum samples (default 5) they create `may_relate` rows instead of gates; with too few samples the source gates and says "not enough data". `agent`, `person`, `learned` and `split` always gate. An `inferred` source whose recall is below its threshold is **bold**: inference also proposes up to five weak links for open globs whose investigated files share a module. A **weak link** is a `relations` row of kind `may_relate` (kinds are now `waits`, `superseded_by`, `may_relate`): every gate, hold, cycle and limit read lists `waits` rows only, so it holds nothing, and it shows in Related under "May relate". `promote_relation` (`POST /api/globs/{id}/relations/{relationId}/promote`, row 34d) turns it into a `waits` relation and records `tp_promoted`; creating a gate for the same pair removes the weak link. **Post-merge check:** a final merge queues `check_relations_after_merge` (`llm` lane) for the merged glob. For each relation it had to a work glob that merged first (no verdict yet, on the same board) it reads both merges' diffs: `needed` when its added lines use a symbol the other added, it imports a file the other created or it changes the same lines of a shared file (reason "uses `X` from s15t246"); `not_needed` when they share no file, import or symbol and the code index could be read; anything else, or an unreadable diff or index, is `unsure` and goes in one batched, size-capped, secret-masked model call (ledger source `relation_check`, `RELATION_CHECK_SCHEMA`) that answers needed, not needed or unsure with a reason. The verdict (`verdict`, `verdictReason`, `verdictBy` `git` or `model`, `verdictAt` on the row) is stored with the signal in one transaction and logged as `RelationVerdict` on the glob; Related shows it on the row. No model or a busy one means the effect waits and retries without spending an attempt; a model `unsure` is neither needed nor not needed. Board settings, Relations (`GET`, admin `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/api/boards/{b}/relation-learning`) shows each source's numbers, window, posture and why; an admin pins a posture, a threshold, the window or the sample minimum, or resets pins (the last 50 changes are kept in `history`). `get_context` carries `relationLearning`, `[{ source, precision, samples, windowDays, posture, note }]` for `agent`, `mention` and `inferred`, and the investigator and orchestrator are told to add a relation only for a concrete dependency. Migration 0084 adds the tables and the verdict columns.

**Needs, overlaps and where they come from (s15f44, s15f127).** A **need** means the glob uses code the other glob adds: it is a start relation and blocks the start (a sub stays in Planning, a same is refused Start, Start now overrides the inferred and learned ones, with a warning; a person's and an old split's keep holding on paper but the glob starts). **Overlaps are no longer edges:** the REST and MCP bodies, split parts and the views carry no `overlaps`. What the board's merge policy and the model predicted about shared files stays on the glob as internal data (`impliedAfter` entries with no kind: `{ id, paths, source? }`) for conflict hotspots and for dependency learning; it never blocks and is not shown. Relations come from a person (`source: person`: `after`, `relations` on `create_glob`/`update_glob`/REST, or `add_relation` and a decision's "Holds back"), an agent (`agent`, the same through MCP, which is also what a part's `after` in `split_glob` becomes), slop's inference from the plan and the other globs (`inferred`, the reason in `reason`; a person can dismiss it, row 34b) and slop's learning (`learned`, below). **Inference produces only start relations:** a need (until merged) on another open glob, and a **decision relation** (start until `decided`) on an open decision glob: one nobody has decided (no accept, reject or closed outcome; needs work is not decided), not archived and, if scoped to one glob, scoped to this one. The model is shown each such decision's statement and scope beside the open work, may name one only as a `decision` and a work glob only as a `needs`, and gives a reason for each. The investigator's `relations` (`{ to, gate?, until?, reason, files? }`; the older `dependencies` list still reads: its needs are start relations, its overlaps are dropped) refine the model's needs and decisions, and only start relations of it are used. A dismissal holds for the plan hash it was made on (`dismissedAt` with `dismissedPlanHash`, never `overriddenAt` alone, which is Start now); a changed plan may infer it again. Only a well-formed model answer (even an empty list) replaces stored inferred relations: a refusal, a cut-off, unparseable JSON or no model keeps them. The plan hash is re-read and the cycle check run inside the writing transaction, under the board's relation lock. A glob that has started holds nothing, so nothing is inferred for it.

**Learned dependency rules (per board).** The path patterns overlaps are predicted from (the merge policy's `exclusivePaths` and the catalog's `dependencies.json`: `overlap` patterns such as migrations folders, and `neverOverlap` patterns such as lockfiles and generated files, which are never predicted) each have a weight in [0, 1] that starts at the catalog default (1, or 0 for never-overlap) and moves with outcomes; below 0.3 a pattern is no longer predicted. A learned weight is a row in `dependency_rules` (board, pattern, weight, clean and conflict counts); a pattern with no row has the catalog default, so a catalog change moves boards that haven't learned. Each move is recorded once per board, glob, pattern and cause in `dependency_rule_changes` (when, pattern, weight before and after, cause, glob, evidence): (1) **clean_overlap**: a glob with an overlap edge merged with no `ConflictFlagged` and no `BehindChanged` that listed files: its patterns drop 0.25 (three clean outcomes stop a default pattern); (2) **conflicted_overlap**: it merged after a conflict or a rebase: +0.5; (3) **unmerged_prerequisite**: `report_failure` text that says prerequisites weren't merged (unmerged, not merged, hasn't merged, blocked on) and names globs on the board: each becomes a learned start relation of the glob (a predicted overlap on it is upgraded, a need already there is confirmed), its patterns +0.5; (4) **start_anyway_succeeded**: an inferred or learned need overridden with Start now whose glob then merged without a failure for it drops to a learned overlap (its relation is removed) and weakens its patterns. The outcomes are applied when the merge is observed (`release_waiting`) and when a failure is reported; neither can fail the transition that caused it. Board settings shows each pattern with its weight, the catalog default, learned or default, and the history; admins reset one pattern or all (`POST /api/boards/{b}/dependency-rules/reset`, `GET /api/boards/{b}/dependency-rules`). Promotion to the catalog or to other boards stays a human `submit_learning` step. Known limits: a failure is recognised by its text, not a structured code; relations without paths (a person's needs) record history but no pattern weight; a glob held in Planning only by an implied hold before this change stays in Planning until Start.

**Sign-off labels** FR (Functional Review), CR (Code Review) and QA are set to Required automatically when a glob moves to Reviewing: subs get QA, sames and supers get FR, CR and QA. After that, only humans change them. Each label has three states and carries a review checklist: **Required** (waiting for the reviewer, initially and whenever the developer resubmits), **Added** (the reviewer added checklist items for the developer to work through) and **Approved** (the reviewer is satisfied, with or without items). While a label is Required the reviewer keeps items as **drafts** on it (saved, edited and removed one at a time; they survive closing the card, a reload and another device, change no label state and notify no one), then **sends** them (the drafts become real items and Required → Added; `submit_items` does the same for items given in the call, promoting any drafts too) or approves (Required or Added → Approved; unsent drafts are discarded, and the board asks for confirmation as "Approve anyway" while items are unticked or drafts exist). Drafts are not items: counts, the state text and the open count ignore them. Sending items never starts or wakes a machine run, for any type; the developer ticks and unticks items while the label is Added and resubmits it for review at any time, even with items unticked (Added → Required, items and ticks kept). Items are never removed, so each review round adds to the same list; once the glob is signed off they are read-only. An approved label can be re-opened (→ Required). Anyone on the board may act on a label for now. The card shows each label as a coloured chip (Required outlined brown, Added clay, Approved green) with a popover for a quick Approve or Re-open; the glob view holds the checklists. The glob lights up when its last label is approved. Who did what and when goes in the event log.

Labels only appear on a card once the glob is merged (Required, Added or Approved).

**plan.md is created with the glob:** `create_glob` (MCP, REST, CLI `slop new`) stores plan.md v1 verbatim, from `plan` if given, else the summary as given, else the intake input, with provenance (human for REST, sessionator for MCP, and the actor). The summary is left as it is. Agents rewrite plan.md with `save_plan`, not `update_glob`. Globs created before this have no plan.md artifact until one is saved; `get_plan` and `get_context` show their summary as version 0.

**Artifacts** (plan.md, the implementation record, local review.md, context attachments) live in slop as versioned records with glob ID, type, version, commit SHA where relevant, and provenance (human, sessionator, or model + prompt version). Binaries go to S3; the glob holds references.

**Implementation plan:** the implementing tool (routine, sessionator or equivalent) generates an implementation plan as an artifact on the glob, with an amendments section. Edits to plan.md after triggering are allowed; the run simply continues, with no lock or special state.

## Board and lifecycle

Globs move through four lists: Planning, Doing, Reviewing, Signed Off. Reviewing means merged to master and awaiting human sign-off; PR review happens while a glob is in Doing.

|  | Planning | Doing | Reviewing | Signed Off |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sub | Skipped | Automatic on creation; routine starts | Automatic when sub review passes and it merges; QA Required | Automatic when all labels are Approved |
| Same | Starts here | Start button or pick-up (glob view or sstor); a routine runs if chosen | Automatic when the developer merges the PR; FR, CR, QA Required. A same with open rollout steps is merged with Merge and continue: it lands the same way and stays in Doing until its steps are done (see Rollout steps) | Automatic when all labels are Approved |
| Super | Skipped | On creation | Automatic when the developer merges the PR; FR, CR, QA Required. Merge and continue lands work mid-way and keeps the glob in Doing | Automatic when all labels are Approved |

**Decision globs** share the four lists but not the work pipelines: a decision is decided in Doing and signed off after (see Decision globs and the knowledge page). The board shows them under the Decisions chip, hides archived ones from the lists and the signed-off counts, and the work lists (`listGlobs` without `include`) never contain them.

**Moves** happen through buttons in the glob view, enabled only where a manual move makes sense. **Type changes** are buttons in the same row, shown only for the changes `changeFields` would accept now (`allowedTypeChanges`): *Make it a sub* (a routine run starts now, or after what it waits for, and it merges itself; it confirms first), *Make it a same* (a person merges it) and *Pair on it* (a super; no routine run). The type is a label beside the status, with the reason no change is possible as its tooltip. Category stays a select offering only what the type allows; it saves on change, shows *Saved* with Undo, and a refused save keeps the old value and shows the reason. Everything else is driven by events, including human events outside slop (a developer merging a PR in GitHub). Manual and automated moves go through the same core transitions.

**Failures:** a failed or timed-out run stays in Doing with a failed flag. No automatic retry. A routine that stops because a person must decide something (not because it lacks effort) calls `report_failure` with `kind: 'needs_pairing'` and the `decision` it would have had to guess: the failure records both, the card says "Needs a person: <decision>", slop never retries it, and a failed sub or same offers **Set type to super** (row 13c) beside Pick up.

**Hand-over brief (s15f103):** when a person picks up or takes over (rows 6-10) or starts a held glob now (row 34a), slop says why they are needed and what to do first. `handoverBrief` (pure, in core) returns `{why, next, detail}` or null from the glob and a few facts the service reads (now, the newest local review's open in-scope classified findings, the phase to resume from, from the uploaded artifacts: none 1, investigation 3, implementation record 4, local review 5, local review of the head 6). Causes, most actionable first, one sentence each in `why`: a conflict with the base branch (or a failed merge on one); a failure (its reason, and a `needs_pairing` decision verbatim); failed head checks (not when they are inherited from a red base branch; the log's first lines, run URL and head SHA go in `detail`); a run that failed, made no progress for the stuck threshold or sat queued over 10 minutes (an investigate-only run and superseded or completed runs never count); open in-scope review findings. `next` is the first cause's step. `get_glob`, `get_context` and the glob view return it as `handover`; the pick-up records it from the glob as it was before the move (a pick-up may clear the failure it explains) as the `Handover` attachment, a new version per hand-over, in the same transaction; no brief, or a no-op pick-up, writes nothing.

**Sub to same conversion** happens when the sub review flags the change; the branch, work and PR are kept, and the developer merges it once satisfied. A sub whose rebase hits a conflict does not convert: it stays in Doing as failed, and the planner finds a human implementer.

**Discard & restart** returns a glob to its starting status (sub: Doing with a new routine run; same: Planning; super: Doing with its creator), closing its PR and deleting its branch (a sub or super is re-provisioned at once; a same gets a new branch when it next starts); see the transition table for everything it resets. **Delete** shows a strong warning, then removes the glob, its branch, its S3 artifacts and its events (so its time leaves the reports). No archive.

**Signed Off** globs drop off the board after 2 weeks (computed on display) and remain in a paginated list view.

**Card corner status (s15f164):** the card has one status, top right, which the glob view's header mirrors exactly (same text, icon and tooltip, both from `presentGlob`'s `status`; the header shows the glob's list by the ID as `s15f164 · Planning`, then on the right `<status> · <category icon>`, only the category icon when the card has no status, and never the list name there). A person's step reads as **one word, never the step's title**: `question` for an Answer step, `do` for a Do step (a rollout or once step, which replaces "rollout") and `decide` for a Decide step; the step's title (a question's title) and "(+N more)" are the tooltip's. In order: a stall, a person's escalation in Planning, Your turn, a problem, a **fanned-out glob's workstream state** while it is being built (`failed · W3` and `waiting · decision for W2`, human icon; `integration conflict · W1+W2` for running workstreams with recorded conflicts; `working · 2/3 workstreams`, done of all, skipped left out), the machine's position, **what holds the glob** (see Relations), other waiting, standby. Once the change is merged (and no deploy of it is still running) with open rollout steps, the corner is **`do`** (no count; human icon; the tooltip says "N of M rollout steps done" and lists the open steps; a click opens the Human steps), in place of "sign off" and "signed off"; when the rollout is done it returns to "sign off" (labels still to approve) or "signed off". A predicted rollout placeholder is not a step here. The server adds a compact `workstreams` (`{ fannedOut, items: [{ name, state, conflicts }] }`, from the glob's latest `WorkstreamsRecorded` event) to the board list and the glob read for a fanned-out glob only; the field is optional. **Arm system label:** a rocket and "Arm system" in the primary action's colour (not red, not the human-step colour), bottom right of the card beside the avatar and in the glob view header beside the list, on a work glob with an open rollout step that was written (not predicted) or an open work step due after the merge, from Planning on; it stays while any such step is open, including a non-blocking `downstream` step and even after sign-off, reads plain "Arm system" with no environment name, and goes when every step is done or dismissed; its tooltip is "A person has a step at release: <first open step>". The Human steps section lists steps that are due in "A step needs you" and the rest as quiet lines (after the merge, on the glob's dev environment, downstream). Clicking it on the board filters to such globs (the filter pane's "Arm system" toggle, URL `arm=1`); in the glob view it links to the board filtered so. There is no split chip on the card ("Split from" stays in the glob view). The status is text and an icon only: a click at most opens the glob view at a section (never an action, never a link), and after a person's click it reads what is happening ("merging", "fixing the conflict", "starting") with the spinning machine, until the glob moves on (see *Presentation*).

**Card display (s15b160):** the card shows exactly: the header (ID · mode on the left; on the right the status with its icon, a dot and the kind icon), the title, the group tag (the shared tag style), the **one primary button, bottom left**, and, bottom right, the Arm system label, a dot and the avatar. The primary button is `presentGlob`'s `cardPrimary`: Pick up, Start, Merge (a first click arms it with "Confirm merge" on the button itself, a second sends it) or Fix conflict (the same), and only when it is the glob view's primary action; Pick up and Start only in Planning, never a Pick up for a failed automatic fix, never Retry, Resume, Take over, Cancel merge, Merge anyway, Deploy, Done or Failed…. There is no loader, pending label, label switches, ATF chip, "PR still draft" chip or assignee chip on the card; the glob view keeps them. A colour shifts from neutral to amber to red as the glob passes 2 calendar days in Doing without merging, and a failed glob has a red border.

**Presentation (s15b160):** one pure function, `presentGlob` (`packages/core/src/domain/presentation.ts`), takes a glob's facts and returns what every screen says: `status` (text and icon for the top right of the card and the glob view's header, an in-progress status after a click included), `primary` (the glob view's one primary action, none while a click is pending or a block holds the glob), `cardPrimary` (that action when it is Pick up, Start, Merge or Fix conflict, else none), `notice`, `blocks` and `activity`. The board card, the glob view's header, action row and Activity log read it through `apps/web/src/lib/present.ts`; no other code works out a status, a button, a problem or a log line. **Blocks** are only Answer (an open question), Decide (a decision or a review label) and Do (a due step a person must carry out, rollout steps included); a conflict, failed checks, a failed run and a stall are never blocks. **The notice** is the one non-dismissable line beside the primary action: how the last click failed (a refusal, a failure or a timeout), else the most important problem, each said once: a conflict with its files (it says the checks re-run once fixed), failed checks only when no conflict explains them, a run failure, a stall, a branch that was not created, a clash with another glob. It is none while a click is pending or a machine run is already working on the problem, and it goes when the next click is sent. There is no separate "looks stuck" line and no Got it. **Pending click:** an action a person clicks that takes effect later (start, pick up, take over, retry, fix conflict, merge, mark ready, prepare, discard) is stored on the glob as `pendingAction` (`kind`, `at`, `by`, the queued `runId`) and shown as in progress, with no primary button, until the glob moves on (its status, PR head, conflict, failure, checks or run change) or 60 seconds pass; a click the machine refused, or that failed or timed out, is stored as `lastOutcome` and is the notice until the next click clears it. The web sends the same shape for the round trip, so the card shows the click at once, and reloads the glob after a refused click (the refusal bumps its version). **Activity:** the PR line says "ready for review" only while there is no open conflict and no failed head checks, else "conflicts with <base>" or "checks failed", and carries the time of the thing it reports; a click and how it ended are entries ("<name> clicked Fix conflict", "Fix conflict failed: <reason>").

**Routine run on the card:** a run indicator shows the current run's state (queued, active, watching, ended: completed, failed or superseded), its routine owner and triggerer, when it started and when it last made progress (its last slop call or push). The glob view lists the run history and, for the current run, an *Open in Claude* link to the cloud session at claude.ai/code and a copyable *Continue locally* command (`sstor --glob <id>`; sstor itself decides to teleport into the run's conversation, see `--teleport` / `--no-teleport` in the sessionator overview). Slop stores each run's cloud session ID and URL from the routine fire response (to verify in slice 4; fallback: the routine reports them on its first MCP call). Only the routine owner can teleport into a run, so only they see *Continue locally*; for everyone else the link is view-only.

**Board bar:** the web app's top bar holds each person's board sessions, like tmux: the boards they keep open, each with its own number like tmux windows. Slop stores, per person and board, the board's number in the bar and when they last opened it; it's personal, never shown to other members, and goes with the membership. Opening a board in the web app (not through MCP) records the time only and never changes the bar: a board joins it through +, taking the lowest free number (1 if free, else the first gap, else one past the highest) (on the *All boards* page, or on the dashed row shown for the current board when it isn't in the bar). × takes a board out and the others keep their numbers, so a gap stays until a new board fills it; a board taken out while open stays shown with the dashed row and its +. The bar lists only sessions; an *All boards* row below it (with *+N more*, the boards not in the bar) links to `/boards`, which lists every board with its counts and last-viewed label, sessions first in number order then the rest by last viewed, each with + to add it to the bar or × to take it out, and *New board*. Number keys 1–9 open the session with that number (a number with no session does nothing); there is no expiry or reordering. `/` opens the board in the bar viewed last, so a board taken out isn't put back; with an empty bar, the board viewed last, else the first board. `GET /api/me` gives each board `position` (its place in the bar, null when it isn't in it) and `lastViewedAt`; `POST /api/boards/{b}/viewed` records an open (the bar is unchanged), `PUT /api/boards/{b}/session` adds a board to the bar at the lowest free number and `DELETE /api/boards/{b}/session` takes it out, each answering with the person's sessions.

**Live updates:** every save publishes a small hint (`kind`, `globId`, `version`) through the notifier port. The server streams it to every open board over server-sent events; the board refetches that glob (unless it already holds that version), and reloads the whole board after reconnecting. The exception is `glob.artifacts`, sent when an artifact is added: artifacts are versioned on their own and the card reads them from the artifact records, so adding one doesn't bump the glob's version (which would turn concurrent client writes into conflicts), and its hint carries no version; the board always refetches that glob, and any open artifact view. Each glob carries a version number and every write is conditional on it, so simultaneous moves resolve cleanly.

**Changing type, category and group:** category and group can be changed at any time (the type/category matrix still applies; IDs keep their original letter). Type changes are limited to what the current execution mode supports: sub to same at any point before merge (the developer then merges instead of auto-merge); sub and super interchangeably only while parked in `planning` (no run, nobody implementing; a super made a sub starts like row 26 and a sub made a super waits for a person); same and super interchangeably in `planning`, `in_progress` or `pr_open` with no run queued, active or watching (a super in Planning offers Pick up but not Start, and picking it up moves it straight to Doing as in row 6; switching back restores Start); same to sub only from `planning`, which is a transition (row 26) and starts work. No other type changes are allowed, and apart from row 26 a type change never moves the glob. Reports use the category at report time.

## State machine

The core tracks a status per glob; the four lists are a projection of it, and every move, manual or automatic, is one of the transitions below.

| Status | List | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `planning` | Planning | Same being planned; nothing running |
| `implementing` | Doing | A routine run is working on the glob |
| `in_progress` | Doing | A human is implementing (supers, or a picked-up glob) |
| `failed` | Doing | A run failed, timed out, hit a merge conflict, or its PR was closed unmerged |
| `pr_open` | Doing | The PR is ready for review, awaiting the sub gate or the developer's merge |
| `merging` | Doing | The glob is in the board's merge line (`mergeLine` is set, see Merging). The head of the line is being updated from the base, rechecked and squash-merged ("merging", or "running checks" while its checks run); the others wait their turn ("next to merge", "N ahead in the merge line") and do no work |
| `reviewing` | Reviewing | On master, awaiting FR/CR/QA sign-off |
| `signed_off` | Signed Off | All required labels Approved |

**Transitions**

| # | From | To | Trigger | Types | Guard | Side effects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | — | `planning` | Create (board, MCP or sstor) | Same | Valid type/category | Intake; planner recorded. No branch yet: it is provisioned when the glob enters Doing |
| 2 | — | `implementing` | Create | Sub (when it has no unmerged `after` or hold: row 32) | Valid type/category | Provision branch, empty first commit and draft PR with labels; queue a routine run for the triggerer (fallback: default routine owner) |
| 3 | — | `planning` | Create as a super (web form, MCP, REST or `sstor --new --super`; `start` is ignored) | Super | Valid type/category | No branch, run or implementer; nobody's active glob. Pick up (row 6) moves it to Doing; it is never started |
| 4 | — | `implementing` | Create with `autoTrigger` | Same | Instruction explicit | As 2 |
| 5 | `planning` | `implementing` | Start (button or `start_glob`) | Same | No start relation of it is unmet (else refused with the reason, row 34 is the override) | Provision as 2; queue a routine run. **While an investigate-only run (row 5a) is queued or active the glob is not started:** it stays in Planning with `waiting` `{since, investigation: {runId, by}}` and the event `StartQueued`; Start and Start now are offered no more and a second one is a no-op. When that run ends (`completed`, or `failed` through `report_failure` or a timeout) the glob starts as above for `by` (`waiting` cleared, event `Released` with the investigation's run ID, run queued with the prerequisite check), and the new run reuses a stored investigation (the orchestrator's reuse rule) or investigates as usual after a failed one. Never a second investigation in parallel |
| 6 | `planning`, `failed` | `in_progress` | Pick up | All (a super parked in Planning (row 3, or a same or sub switched to super there) is picked up here to pair, never started, and the card's button reads "Pick up to pair"; a glob held by row 32 is picked up here too, with a warning naming what hasn't merged, `waiting` cleared and the `PickedUp` event listing it) | No run active or watching (an investigate-only run never counts) | Implementer = picker; provision as 2 if the glob has no branch yet; a queued run is cancelled together with its launch job, **except a live investigate-only run, which keeps going**: `waiting` (a queued Start included) is cleared, the pick-up reply warns that an investigation has been running since a time (its session link), `get_context` carries `investigationRunning` `{runId, since, sessionUrl}` until it ends, and the orchestrator waits for it (polling `get_glob` at most every 2 minutes, up to 20) and reuses its result, or goes on after the timeout and says so in the record. The run still ends by `investigationPushed` or `report_failure` on the picked-up glob. A pick-up that changes the glob (also a take-over, and Start now row 34a) records the hand-over brief (below) as a `Handover` attachment. |
| 7 | `pr_open` | `pr_open` | Pick up | All | No run active or watching | Implementer = picker; status unchanged; a queued run is cancelled with its launch job. A pick-up that changes the glob (also a take-over, and Start now row 34a) records the hand-over brief (below) as a `Handover` attachment. |
| 8 | `in_progress`, `pr_open` | unchanged | Pick up by the current implementer | All | — | No-op (idempotent), unless it chooses a different environment, which is then recorded as a field change |
| 9 | `in_progress`, `pr_open` | unchanged | Pick up by someone else | All | No run active or watching | Implementer changes; a queued run is cancelled with its launch job. A pick-up that changes the glob (also a take-over, and Start now row 34a) records the hand-over brief (below) as a `Handover` attachment. |
| 9a | `reviewing` | unchanged | Pick up on a merged glob that is open for its rollout steps (s15t228) | All except QA and PO on a same or super | The PR is merged, at least one rollout step holding back sign-off is open (`facts.rolloutSteps`), and no run is active or watching (a signed-off glob, or one with no open steps, is refused) | Implementer becomes the person; event `PickedUp`; no status change, nothing is superseded (the merge ended the run); the card and the glob view show `sstor --glob <id>` and Open in Claude as for any pick up. A pick up by the glob's own implementer is a no-op |
| 10 | `implementing`, `pr_open` | `in_progress` (from `implementing`) or unchanged | Pick up (`pick_up`; an active run also needs `takeOver`, otherwise it is refused with `run_active`) | Sub, same | Run queued, active or watching | Run superseded silently when queued or on standby, only with `takeOver` when active (the CLI asks first, the web click confirms) (a queued run's launch job is cancelled); generation increased; implementer = picker. A pick-up that changes the glob (also a take-over, and Start now row 34a) records the hand-over brief (below) as a `Handover` attachment. |
| 11 | `implementing`, `in_progress` | `pr_open` | Draft PR marked ready for review | All | PR head branch = glob ID | Record PR; an active run moves to watching |
| 12 | `pr_open` | `merging` | The PR is ready and the board's sub policy (size, sensitive paths) passes on the head commit | Sub | Type is still sub and no `merge` relation of it is unmet (else row 14a) | Slop squash-merges at once, without waiting for the PR's checks; they run on the base branch after the merge (row 16b) |
| 13 | `pr_open` | `pr_open` | The sub policy flags the change | Sub | — | Type becomes same; the developer now merges |
| 13a | `planning`, `implementing`, `in_progress`, `pr_open` | unchanged | `escalate_to_same` (a routine with its run ID, or a person) | Sub | Run ID is current when given; a reason is given | Type becomes same in place (same branch and PR, no auto-merge); `escalation` (reason, evidence, who, when) is recorded and shown on the card as "Needs a human"; counts as needing attention. A superseded run's call is ignored; a same is left as it is |
| 13b | `implementing`, `in_progress`, `pr_open` | unchanged | Set type to sub (board, `deescalate`) | Same with an escalation; refused (`invalid_transition`, "use Merge and continue") for a `risk_path` escalation while rollout steps are open | — | Type becomes sub, escalation cleared; on a ready PR whose head checks passed the sub gate runs on the head |
| 13c | `failed` | `in_progress` | Set type to super (board, `make-super`) | Sub, same (not bugs) | The failure is `kind: 'needs_pairing'` (the routine's `report_failure` said a person must decide something); the actor is a developer or admin, not QA or PO; no run active or watching | Type becomes super, implementer = the person, failure cleared; a still-queued run is superseded with cause `make_super`; branch, PR and provisioning stay; events `FieldsChanged` (type) and `PickedUp` (`madeSuper`); recorded as a type correction. Pick up stays available (sames and subs can always be picked up) |
| 13d | `planning`, `implementing`, `in_progress`, `pr_open` | unchanged | A rollout step that holds back sign-off was written (a person's `add_human_step`, the implementation record's `## Rollout` section, or the rollout check's answer): `rolloutStepsAdded`, run by the service after each such write (s15t228) | Sub (type change); same or super (arming only) | The glob has at least one open `rollout` work step that blocks sign-off (the investigator's placeholder doesn't count) | A sub becomes a same in place: `escalation` `{ criterion: risk_path, reason: "Has rollout steps: a person merges it with Merge and continue", by: slop }`, `mergeHold` cleared, events `FieldsChanged` (type) and `Escalated`, labels resynced; an armed merge-when-checks-pass is disarmed (`MergeDisarmed`, reason `rollout_steps`). A glob that is already a same, or a sub that has merged, is left as it is here (a merged sub is converted when a step fails, row 31a). Idempotent, so a repeat changes nothing. The sub gate and the sub merge never see a sub with steps, because the conversion happens when the steps are written (and, for a merged sub that gained steps, when one fails) |
| 14 | `pr_open` | `merging` | Merge button on the glob (one Merge for everyone: a same with open rollout steps uses it too) | Same, super | Checks pass on the current head and no `merge` relation of the glob is unmet (else refused with "Can't merge until s15f82 is merged"; also `merge_continue`, and retrying a failed merge) | The glob joins the board's merge line (`mergeLine: {joinedAt}`, event `MergeLineJoined`, `advance_merge_line` queued; see Merging). Pressing Merge is the whole action: nothing more is offered bottom left of the card. When it is the head of the line (`MergeLineHead`), slop squash-merges it (a conflict found while updating the branch returns it to `pr_open`, row 16); while it waits behind another glob it is not updated and its checks are not rerun. **A same with open rollout steps (s15t228, one Merge since s15b158)** is merged with plain Merge once the head's checks passed (never armed), which squash-merges as above with `mergeMode` left null (the record at the head is a super's rule only), so the merge is observed as row 15: status `reviewing`, labels Required, the steps due, listed in Doing and "Merged; rollout N/M" until they are done. `merge_continue` is a super's checkpoint merge (Merge and continue) and an alias of Merge for one release for a same. A super keeps both, whatever its steps |

Failing checks fail it with "Checks failed after updating the branch" when slop updated the branch for this merge (`mergeUpdates` > 0), else "Checks failed on the run's push" (or "on <implementer>'s push"), `failure.kind: 'merge'` (see row 16a); a head with no check result after the stuck time fails with "No check result N minutes after <update|push>; recheck or push", also `failure.kind: 'merge'`. Whatever the outcome, the glob leaves the line (`mergeLine` cleared) with its reason, the existing failure or conflict UI says it, and `advance_merge_line` is queued so the next glob in the line becomes the head. The glob view warns on Merge, before it is pressed, when the base has moved since the branch caught up (`behind`) and no conflict is recorded |

Sames and supers are never reverted: the red base shows on the board and a person decides |

Merge comes back |

| 31a | `reviewing` | `implementing`, or `in_progress` when a person implements | A rollout step failed: `rolloutStepFailed`, from the glob view's "It failed" form, the `fail_human_step` tool or `POST /globs/{id}/steps/{stepId}/fail` (s15t228), which route a rollout step of a merged glob here and any other step to the fix-glob suggestion (s15b148) | Developer, admin; not QA or PO on a same or super (`restrictedFrom`) | The PR is merged and the glob is in `reviewing` (not signed off), and the step is the glob's own rollout step, open or done (a dismissed one is refused); the actor is a member | The glob's next generation starts, as Start again (row 23) does for an unmerged one: `generation` + 1, live runs ended (`superseded`, cause `rollout_step_failed`), the merged PR (number, merge SHA from the last `Merged` event, time) is pushed onto `prs` (so a late "merged" observation of it is the no-op of row 15), and `pr`, `headChecks`, `labels`, `checklists`, `failure`, `mergeMode`, `conflict`, `behind` and `mergeHold` are cleared and `provisioning` is `none`. Effects, in order: `delete_branch` (new generation; the old branch is cut again from the base), then, through entering Doing, `provision` (fresh branch, new draft PR), then `fire_routine` for the actor. When a person implements the glob (`implementer` set, or a super, whose implementer is its creator when none is set) the status is `in_progress` with no run instead. A glob that is still a `sub` (it merged and then gained steps from the rollout check) becomes a same in the same transition, exactly as row 13d (escalation `risk_path`, `mergeHold` cleared, events `FieldsChanged` and `Escalated`), so the next generation never goes through the sub gate. Event `RolloutStepFailed` `{ step, title, generation }`. The service (`failRolloutStep`) stores the actor's note, **masked** by `maskSecrets` (AWS keys and tokens, bearer and basic authorization, GitHub tokens, `password=`/`token=`/`secret=` values, PEM blocks, credentials in URLs) and cut at 8,000 characters, as an `attachment` artifact labelled `Rollout step failed (generation N)` (N the generation that failed) with the step's title and commands, which the next run reads in `get_context` as in-scope feedback; it reopens a done step and logs `HumanStepChanged` (`failed`) without the note. The step stays open: pending until the next generation merges (Merge and continue again), due again after it, and sign-off waits. A separate glob is for a fix that belongs to another board or repo |

An oversized flag never holds it | No branch, no PR, no run. `waiting` set, event `Waiting` (`for`, `note`). A same with only a start relation and no start request has no `waiting` and is simply refused Start (row 5). Predicted overlaps (the merge policy) never hold it | An id on a `needs`/`after` list that was ignored is reported in `ignoredAfter`; a merged glob with due rollout steps is not ignored (it keeps holding, see Relations).
