| 33 | `planning` (held) | `implementing` | Release: a glob it waited for merged (row 15 queues the `release_waiting` effect in the same transaction); the effect's executor starts each glob in Planning with `waiting` and a start relation to the merged glob whose relations are all met (a deletion of the glob they waited for does the same, row 33 is run for each), one version-checked write each; it then runs `gateReleased` for every glob with a relation to the merged glob, whatever its gate (rows 14b and 21b). The same effect is queued when a decision is accepted, rejected or closed and when a glob is signed off | Sub, same | `waiting` set and no start relation unmet; the glob's predicted overlaps are checked again just before and recorded (they never hold it) | `waiting` cleared, event `Released`; provision as 2, so the branch is cut from the current base; queue a run for the glob's creator. Idempotent (a second delivery or a retry changes nothing) and generation-checked; only the final merge releases, never a Merge and continue | A relation to a merged glob releases only once that glob's due rollout steps are done or dismissed.
| 5a | `planning` | `planning` | Investigate (board, REST `investigate`, `investigate_glob`), or slop itself when intake flagged the glob big or its part count is a text-only guess for a plan of more than one task and the board's `autoInvestigate` is on (the default), once per glob | Sub, same (not QA/PO on a same) | A sub or same in Planning with no run queued, active or watching | Queue a routine run with purpose `investigate` for the caller (the creator when automatic); the glob keeps its status and gets no branch or PR (no `provision`). The run does the orchestrator's Phases 1–2 only. It ends `completed` when an `investigation` whose block validates is stored with its run ID; `report_failure` or a timeout (no-progress hours, queued-start limits) ends it `failed` and leaves the glob in Planning. Starting never supersedes it: Start queues behind it (row 5). Picking up leaves it running (row 6). Discarding (Start again) or deleting the glob still ends it as superseded. On a picked-up glob the run watch still times it out (`report_failure` path) |
| 34 | `planning` (held, or a same with an unmerged `after`) | `implementing` | Start now (board, which asks for an optional reason; `start_glob` with `anyway` and an optional `reason`) | Sub, same | Something holds it: an unmet start relation (an oversized flag holds nothing, so there is no Start now for it) | `waiting` cleared, the inferred, learned and mention start relations get `overriddenAt` (a person's, an agent's and an old split's are left), event `HoldOverridden` (with the `reason` when given); provision and queue a run as 2 | Start now never overrides a relation a person or an agent set.
| 34a | `in_progress` | `implementing` | Start now, shown as Start (board, `start_glob` with `anyway`) | Sub, same | A person is implementer, no routine run is queued, active or watching | Implementer cleared, generation bumped, a run queued for the caller. Picking up is never a side effect of moving a card: Start now is the board's default move for a held glob, Pick up its own explicit move |
| 34b | any work glob | unchanged | Dismiss relation (glob view ✕ "Not a dependency", `POST /api/globs/{id}/relations/{relationId}/dismiss`, `dismiss_relation`) | Any member | The relation exists on the glob (an old split's start relation too); a repeat is a no-op | Relation gets `dismissedAt` and `dismissedPlanHash` (hash of the latest plan), event `RelationDismissed` (with its gate and source); a sub held only by that need starts (release), a merge or sign-off held only by it goes on (`gateReleased`). A dismissal that matches the current plan hash is not re-inferred; on a merge, a dismissed `inferred` start relation to a work glob weakens its covering patterns (cause `edge_dismissed`; a decision, merge or sign-off relation teaches nothing) |
| 34c | any work glob | unchanged | Add relation (glob view "Add relation…", `POST /api/globs/{id}/relations` with `to`, `add_relation`; a decision's "Holds back…" with `holdsBack`; or `relations` on `create_glob`/`update_glob`) | Any member | The other glob is on the board, is not this one, the shape makes sense (`decided` only for a decision glob, `done` only for an action glob and a relation to an action is only `done`; a decision glob is never the one held back, and an action is held back only by a start relation), no cycle through any gate, at most 20 per glob and gate, a reason of at most 300 characters; with `to` the glob's version must match | Relation row (source `person`, or `agent` through MCP) written under the board's relation lock with the event `RelationAdded`, the held glob's version moves. A relation already set by a person or agent to the same glob and gate only changes its `until` and reason; an inferred or learned one, or one dismissed or overridden, gives way to the new one; the glob that is held back is the one written (for a decision's "Holds back" no version is needed) | Since s15b148 a relation may point at a glob on another board (see Cross-board relations), and `supersedes` is a relation kind too (see Supersedes); an agent relation needs a reason, and one slop adds from a mention is `source: mention`.
| 34d | any work glob | unchanged | Make a weak link a gate (glob view "Make it a gate" under "May relate", `POST /api/globs/{id}/relations/{relationId}/promote` with `version`, `promote_relation`) | Any member | A `may_relate` row of the glob that is not dismissed; `to` still holds (not yet at `until`); a start gate only while the glob is in Planning; no gate for the pair; no cycle and under the limit; the version matches | The row becomes `waits` with the same gate, until and reason, event `RelationAdded` (note "promoted from a weak link"), signal `tp_promoted`, then `gateReleased`. A weak link can also be dismissed (row 34b; no signal) |
| Q1 | any work glob | unchanged (a sub in Planning gets `waiting`) | Raise a question (`raise_question`, `POST /api/globs/{id}/questions`, intake on create, escalation, a plan.md saved after a run started) | Any member; slop itself | Not a decision or action glob; text 1–1000 characters, at most 6 options, at most 20 questions per glob; a `key` the glob already has (open, answered or dismissed) changes nothing; a `merge` question is refused once the glob has merged; a routine's `runId` must be the glob's current run, or the call is ignored; from an agent or routine, text with a code span holding a shell command and no options beyond done/failed is refused (`invalid_input`: a command to run is a human step, not a question) | `questions` gains `{id: q<n>, key?, text, options?, raisedBy, source, blocking, raisedAt}`, event `QuestionRaised`. `blocking`: `start` holds a glob in Planning (a start gate beside the start relations: Start is refused and hidden, Start now stays; a sub gets `waiting`; created with one, a sub is created held with no run); on a glob that has started, or a super, a `start` question is stored as `none`; `merge` holds Merge, merge-when-green, the sub gate and Retry merge (an armed or sub-gated merge records `mergeHold.questions`, `MergeHeld`) while open, and is a notice while the merge is under way; `none` is a notice. An escalation to a same also attaches its reason as a `none` question (`escalation:<criterion>`). plan.md saved by a person during a run (or after it, before the merge) queues `plan_changed_question`, which raises a `merge` question (`plan:v<N>`: "plan.md changed to vN during the run; the run used vM") |
| Q2 | any work glob | unchanged (`implementing` for a held sub, `merging` for a held merge, through rows 33 and 14b) | Answer a question (`answer_question`, `POST /api/globs/{id}/questions/{qid}/answer`) | Any member (QA and PO too) | The question is open; the answer has text or one of the options; a call naming one of the glob's own run IDs is refused (`forbidden`: a routine never answers); the same answer again changes nothing, another is refused | `answer {text, option?, by, at}`, event `QuestionAnswered`; in the same transaction the question and answer are appended to the `Clarifications` attachment (a new version, an existing label of any case kept), so `get_context` and choice extraction read it. Then a `start` question runs `release` (a held sub starts, queueing its run; a held same stays in Planning with Start offered again) and a `merge` question runs `gateReleased` (a held merge resumes) |
| Q3 | any work glob | as Q2 | Dismiss a question (`dismiss_question`, `POST /api/globs/{id}/questions/{qid}/dismiss`) | Any member | As Q2 | `dismissed {by, at, note}`, event `QuestionDismissed`; a one-line "go ahead as is" entry (with the note) goes into Clarifications; the same release as Q2 |
| Q4 | `planning` (held by a start question) | `implementing` | Start now (row 34) | Sub, same | An open start question holds it | The open start questions get `startOverridden {by, at}` and stay open; event `HoldOverridden` lists them. Picking the glob up by hand (rows 6) does the same |
| 35 | `in_progress` | unchanged | A push to a glob in Doing with a PR queues `check_exclusive_paths` (not for a superseded run's push or the `<id>: start` commit); its executor compares the branch with the base | All | — | `clash` set (`{ with, paths }`, event `ClashChanged`) while the branch changes an exclusive path that another open glob's branch changes too, cleared when it no longer does or when that glob merges. A warning on the card only: it blocks nothing |
| 35a | any open | unchanged | A merge, a push to an open glob's branch, a status change, a deletion or a plan save queues one `recheck_overlaps` per board (a pending one is reused; it runs a few seconds later, so a burst runs once); its executor recomputes every open glob's predicted overlap edges | System | — | Edges that no longer hold drop off (the other glob merged, was deleted, went back to Planning or failed without a branch, or no longer changes an exclusive path), new ones are added (events `OverlapDropped`, `OverlapAdded`, with the reason); needs (relations), learned and overridden edges are kept. A plan predicts an exclusive path only from its Tasks or Files sections (or whole, when it has no headings) |
| 36 | pr_open (same, super); reviewing (sub) | unchanged | A same or super marked ready, and each push while its PR is ready, queues one `review_change` (pre-merge) for the PR head; a sub's merge queues one (post-merge) for the squash commit. The outbox keeps one job per glob, stage and commit, and a newer head cancels the older pending pre-merge job; the executor also drops a job whose head is no longer current. Never blocks merging | System | — | A board whose reviewer is off (`review.enabled`, on by default) drops its automatic `review_change` jobs in the executor, those queued before it was turned off too; Review code still works. One independent review per reviewed commit; the runner assembles the input, reviews it (findings and verification passes), stores the `independent_review` artifact and publishes it (see Publishing below) |
| 36a | any status in Doing, or `reviewing`, with an open or merged PR, no live run, and either a missing automatic review or a person implementing a same or super | unchanged | **Review code** (`POST /globs/{id}/actions/review-now`, MCP `review_now`, the glob view's button; any board member, no version check). It is allowed (and the API refuses otherwise with `invalid_transition`) only when no machine run is live and either (a) the automatic review for the glob's stage is missing at the current head (a ready same without a `pre_merge` review at the PR head, any merged glob without a `post_merge` review): the button reads **Retry review** and may lead when nothing else does; or (b) a person implements a same or super with an open PR: "Review code" in the ▾ only, never the main button. A sub in Doing, or a same while the machine implements, never offers it. The glob view shows a sub's Pick up as an outline main while its machine run progresses and black once it is stalled. It queues one `review_change` with a `requestId`. It is never deduplicated against, nor cancelled by, the automatic jobs (the outbox dedupe and the newer-head cancel apply only to jobs without a `requestId`), so every request runs. Its executor reviews the PR as it is when it runs: an open PR (draft or ready) at the pre-merge stage on the host's test-merge ref, a merged one at the post-merge stage on the squash commit; only a PR closed without merging, or a test merge that conflicts, drops it, and a test merge the host hasn't computed yet is retried | Any | PR is open or merged; glob in Doing or Reviewing; no live run; a missing automatic review or a person's own same or super | Event `ReviewRequested`; the glob is unchanged. The run is queued, not awaited: the request answers at once |

**Rules across all transitions**

- Every command and event loads the glob's current state and is checked against this table; anything not listed is refused.
- Writes are conditional on the glob's version, so simultaneous events resolve cleanly.
- Changing type, category or group is not a transition: it never moves the glob, only changes which rows apply next.
- Draft PRs are recorded on the glob without changing status.
- Sub policy (rows 11–13): a sub is evaluated against the board's policy as soon as it enters `pr_open`; its checks are not awaited. The `checks` workflow also runs on every push to the base branch, which is how a red base (and a sub that caused it, row 16b) is found.
- Time attribution follows the planner until a human implementer picks the glob up, then the implementer; triggeredBy only chooses whose routine runs.

**Provisioning and reliability**

- **Provisioning:** a glob gets its branch, empty first commit and draft PR when it enters Doing: at creation for subs and auto-started sames; on Start or Pick up for a same in Planning, and on Pick up for a super, which always waits in Planning. Planning stays free of GitHub, and nobody can work on a branch before the glob is started or picked up. Provisioning is queued before any routine run, and a routine is only fired once its glob is provisioned. `create_glob` takes an idempotency key and returns once provisioning has been attempted (`provisioning: none` for a same in Planning). If GitHub fails, the glob is still saved with a provisioning-failed flag and the response returns its ID and provisioning status, so a retry with the same key never creates a second glob; a background job retries, and sstor reports the problem instead of checking anything out. A 404 or 403 from the code host is not retried: the glob's `failure` (kind `provisioning`) says why and how to fix it (install the App on the repo, or add `contents: write`), its queued run ends with the same reason, the card and glob view show it first, and the board's bar warns "slop's GitHub App can't access <repo>" (source `repo-access`) until the next successful code host call for that repo. Other failures retry with backoff, and the last attempt records the failure the same way.
- **Routine runs** have their own lifecycle, independent of the glob's status: queued → active (the routine has called slop) → watching (its PR is ready and auto-fix is running) → ended (completed, failed, superseded). A queued run that makes no slop call within the board's start time (default 30 minutes) never started: it is failed with the reason "Routine run never started (queued at `<time>`)", and the card shows that reason with the run's session link and Discard & restart. While it is queued the card shows how long once that passes 10 minutes ("Routine run queued for 25 min: open the session"). Runs that never start count toward the board's routines readiness item when it repeats (two recent failures). A run ends when the glob is merged, picked up, taken over, discarded, closed or deleted. Each run has a run ID, passed in the fire payload and returned with its artifacts and `report_failure`; results carrying a superseded run ID are recorded but ignored.
- **Generation:** each glob has a generation number, increased by re-trigger, take over and start again. Every outbox job carries the generation it was queued under and is re-checked before it executes; jobs from an older generation are cancelled.
- **Commit-specific results:** check, review, build and ATF results are tied to a commit SHA; results for commits that are no longer the branch head do not drive transitions.
- **Deduplication:** GitHub deliveries are deduplicated by delivery ID, other webhooks by their source event ID. Incoming events are applied against the glob's current version inside the handler; only client writes carry an expected version.
- **Outbox:** side effects (firing routines, GitHub provisioning, CodeBuild deploys, `release_waiting` after a merge, `check_relations_after_merge` after a merge (judges the merged glob's relations, `llm` lane), `advance_merge_line` (board-wide, not generation-checked: it picks the next head of a board's merge line when a glob joins or leaves it), a decision being decided or a glob being signed off, `check_exclusive_paths` after a push, `check_rollout_paths` after a final merge, `recheck_overlaps` after a merge, push or status change, `commit_agent_set` ten minutes after the last agent-set version bump, `review_change` when a PR is marked ready, pushed to while ready, a sub merges, or Review code is asked) are written to an outbox table in the same transaction as the state change and executed by the job queue with retries and idempotency keys, so partial failures resume.
- **Outbox lanes:** every effect kind has a lane (`laneOf(kind)` in core, stored in `outbox.lane` at insert). Each lane has its own `OutboxRunner`, so a slow lane never holds up another: every kind is in `default` except the KB pipeline's routing and drafting (`kb_route`, `kb_draft`) and `check_relations_after_merge`, which go in `llm` because a model call can take a minute or wait out an "LLM busy" backoff. A lane keeps the outbox's guarantees (written with the state change, re-checked by the executor, retried with backoff: 5 s doubling on `default`, 30 s doubling on `llm`, capped at an hour), holds a claimed job for its lease (the backoff on `default`; at least 5 minutes on `llm`, so a second runner never takes a step mid-call) and has its own attempt limit (`maxAttemptsOf`: 8 for `default`, 3 for `llm`, the KB pipeline's `MAX_PROCESSING_ATTEMPTS`). An executor may return `{ state: 'pending', retryAfterMs, reason? }` to run again later without spending an attempt; `reason` is kept as the job's `last_error` (without one the last error stays). The `llm` runner tells the board's Knowledge page (`board.kb`) when a KB job fails, fails an attempt or waits for a new reason; a finished job's own write already told it, and a wait that repeats its reason is no news. `drain(globId)` callers (create_glob) drain only `default`. The `default` runner starts with the `outbox` job switch and the `llm` runner with its own, `llm`, or with `kb`, so `SLOP_JOBS=kb` (and `SLOP_SESSION_JOBS=kb`) still means KB processing and a later llm-lane kind isn't skipped where `kb` is off.
- **Routine ownership:** slop stores `triggeredBy` and `routineOwner` separately. `triggeredBy` chooses whose routine runs; commits, PRs and Claude usage belong to the routine owner (e.g. the board's default routine owner on fallback); time follows the planner until a human picks the glob up.
- **Routines and humans (cooperative):** pick-up is refused while a run is active or watching; Take over supersedes it. Routines check the glob through the MCP before every push and stop if their run is superseded, a human implementer is recorded, or the glob is merged. Because the check and the push are separate operations, a late push is still possible: routine commits carry a `Slop-Run: <runId>` trailer, and a push from a superseded run is flagged on the glob so the developer can revert it. Slop deletes any glob branch recreated by a late push after merge (to `reviewing`; a super's Merge and continue keeps its branch, row 31).

**Flags shown alongside status** (not states): failure reason, ATF failure, branch-deploy state, aging colour, "PR still draft".

**Domain events** written to the log: `GlobCreated`, `FieldsChanged`, `StatusChanged` (from, to, actor), `RunTriggered`, `RunFailed`, `BranchCreated`, `CommitPushed`, `PROpened`, `PRReadyForReview`, `PRClosed`, `SubReviewCompleted`, `ReviewReceived`, `Merged`, `MergeLineJoined`, `MergeLineHead`, `MergeFailed`, `LabelChanged`, `LabelItemTicked`, `PickedUp`, `ArtifactAdded`, `WorkstreamsRecorded` (a changed workstreams block in the record, s15f146), `DeployRequested`, `DeployReplaced`, `DeployStarted`, `Deployed`, `DeployFailed`, `BuildCompleted`, `ATFCompleted`, `GlobDeleted`.

**Run failure detection (row 17)** uses both mechanisms: the agent calls `report_failure` when it recognises a failure, and slop fails a run that shows no progress (no slop call or push) for a per-board number of hours (default 2), or that has not marked its PR ready within a longer per-board limit (default 8 hours). A watching run (auto-fix) is idle by design, so it has **no no-progress limit**: a watcher on a PR whose head checks pass or are pending is never failed for being quiet. It is failed only for not responding: when the head's checks fail (not inherited from the base) the clock starts, and a watcher that makes no slop call or push within the board's response window (`runRespondMinutes`, default 30 minutes; counted from the failure or the run's last call, whichever is later) is failed with the reason "Auto-fix didn't respond to failed checks (`<check>`) on `<sha>`". The glob stays in pr\_open, the card shows that reason, and offers **Retry auto-fix** (row 25a) beside Pick up. **One automatic retry:** the first time a watcher gives up this way, slop does the Retry auto-fix itself instead of showing the failure: a new routine run on the same branch and PR at the same generation (marked as the automatic retry, so a person sees both runs in the history), whose fire text carries the failure summary (the failing check's name, step and first lines). Only when that run also ends without fixing the PR does the card show the reason and the **Retry auto-fix** button. A run that reports another failure itself, a super, and a glob with a human implementer are not retried automatically. Review comments aren't tracked by slop yet, so an ignored comment doesn't start the clock. Watching ends when the run ends. The routine's session must subscribe to the PR's activity after `mark_ready`; the orchestrator's Auto-fix section says how.

## Contracts

Slop has four kinds of interface: MCP tools for agents and the Claude app, REST for the board, inbound webhooks and events, and outbound calls. All of them call the same application services and enforce the same state machine and role rules.

**Common rules**

- **Auth:** the board uses a server-side session after Cognito login; MCP and REST accept Cognito access tokens, so every call acts as a real person with their board role; webhooks and ingest are verified by per-integration secrets or signatures.
- **Versions:** every client write to a glob carries the version it read; a stale version returns a conflict with the current glob. Exceptions: put\_artifact and report\_failure are append-style records guarded by run ID (for routines) rather than glob version, since artifacts are versioned on their own and a failure report must not be lost to a concurrent edit.
- **Errors:** `forbidden` (role), `not_found`, `version_conflict`, `invalid_transition` (with current status and allowed actions), `invalid_combination` (type/category matrix).

**MCP tools**

| Tool | Input | Returns | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `whoami` | — | user, boards, role per board | Sessionator, Claude app |
| `create_glob` | board, input, idempotencyKey; optional title, summary, plan, type, category (`feature` (stored as a task), `task`, `bug` or `action`; an action takes no type, environment, start, files or inbox item, and its `assignee` is the email of the person meant to do it), assignee, group, environment, links, autoTrigger, `needs`/`after` (glob IDs to start after; row 32), `reason` (one line applied to every id in `needs`/`after`), `relations` (`[{ to, gate?, until?, reason }]`: any gate, source `agent`, the reason required; row 34c), `holds` (alias `follows_up`: existing globs on any board that the new glob holds back; gate default `sign_off`; reason required), `supersedes` (globs this one replaces; reason required) | `{ id, version, branch, `after`, `waitingFor`, `ignoredAfter`, `holds`, `typeNote`, provisioning: none \| ok \| failed, status, type, category, group, environment, summary, after?, waiting?, suggestedAfter? }` (`suggestedAfter`: globs the request said to wait for, from intake; never applied) (same glob returned for a repeated key) | All |
| `split_glob` | id, version, idempotencyKey, parts (title, plan, optional category, type, `after` as indexes of earlier parts it needs, `attachments` labels; no `overlaps`) | `{ parts: [{ part, id, version, title, type, category, status, after?, waiting? }] }`, part 0 being the original (same parts returned for a repeated key) | Agents, board |
| `get_glob` | id | full glob: status, version, generation, fields, labels, `relations` (`holding`, `holdsBack`, `done`; see Relations), `holding`, `waitingFor`, `waitedOnBy`, PR, PRs merged with Merge and continue, current run (state, runId, owner, triggeredBy, started, last progress, cloud session ID and URL), run history, flags, artifact list, latest deploys (environment, commit, state, error, log link; super mode reports them after each push), and `handover` (the hand-over brief, null when nothing is wrong) | All |
| `get_context` | id; optional include | assembled context bundle with citations: the plan in full (also as `payload`), other artifacts listed, and for a decision glob its `attributes`; `scopedDecisions` lists the accepted decision globs that govern the glob (see Context assembly and Decision globs; the field name is kept); `decisions` lists the glob's choices (name kept); `handover` is the hand-over brief (null when nothing is wrong) | Routines, sessionator |
| `get_artifact` | id, kind, optional label | one artifact's latest version in full | Agents |
| `list_globs` | board; optional status, type, `category` (without it: code work; `action` or `decision` list human work, whose `type` is null), group, person | glob summaries | Claude app |
| `complete_action` | id, version; optional `note` (what was checked) | the action glob, Signed Off with outcome `done`; releases the globs waiting for it; a repeat is unchanged, after a dismissal `invalid_transition` | Board members |
| `dismiss_action` | id, version, `reason` (required) | the action glob, Signed Off with outcome `dismissed`; releases the globs waiting for it; a repeat is unchanged, after done `invalid_transition` | Board members |
| `get_group` | board, name | the group view: globs, progress, relations, open decisions | Claude app |
| `update_glob` | id, version; optional title, summary, type, category, group, environment, `needs`/`after` with `reason` (only while the glob has no branch; replaces the start list), `relations` (any gate, reason required; added or changed, never removed), `supersedes` | updated glob, with the same `after`, `waitingFor`, `ignoredAfter`, `typeNote` | All |
| `add_relation` | id; `to` with version (the glob is held back until `to` reaches `until`) or `holdsBack` (the other glob is held back until this one does); optional gate, until, `supersedes` (this glob replaces `to`), reason (required for an agent; row 34c); `to` may be on another board | the glob's view | Agents, board |
| `promote_relation` | id, version, relationId (row 34d) | the glob's view: the weak link ("may relate") is now a gate | Agents, board |
| `dismiss_relation` | id, version, relationId (row 34b; also dismisses an automatic relation, which is remembered) | the glob's view | Agents, board |
| `fail_human_step` | id, stepId, note, version? | the step; the note is masked. A rollout step of a merged glob in `reviewing` starts the glob's next generation (row 31a) and comes back reopened. Any other step stays open and is not marked, a `HumanStepFailed` event records the note and a card suggestion "this step failed: is there a fix glob?" is left | Agents, board |
| `save_plan` | id, version (the plan version read; 0 when none is saved yet), content | the new plan.md version (`version_conflict` if plan.md has moved on); the same write as `PUT /globs/{id}/plan` | All |
| `attach` | id, version, text or link, label | attachment reference | All |
| `ingest_text` | board, text, title; optional glob id | communication id | Claude app |
| `add_to_inbox` | board, text; optional title, occurredAt (ISO date), sourceLabel | `{ id, duplicate }`: the pasted text is stored and searchable at once and appears in the board's inbox; the same text again returns the existing item (`duplicate: true`). Any member of the board (as slice 9, part 1 built it) | Claude app |
| `start_glob` | id, version; optional anyway, optional reason (logged with Start now) | updated glob (same: `planning` → `implementing`; refused with the reason while it waits for an unmerged glob; `anyway` is Start now, row 34) | Claude app |
| `pick_up` | id, version; optional takeOver, environment | updated glob (with the environment, if given, and `warnings` naming the globs a held glob was still waiting for; the brief is recorded as a `Handover` attachment when the pick-up changes the glob), or `run_active` if a run is active or watching and takeOver is not set | Sessionator, Claude app |
| `put_artifact` | id, kind (`implementation_plan`, the implementation record; `postplan` is an alias that writes it; `local_review`, or `investigation`), content; optional commitSha, runId, agentSetVersion, and for a local review reviewStats (risk tier, review rounds run and allowed, tester FAIL→fix loops; kept in its provenance for mined signals) | artifact version (ignored if runId is superseded); for an `investigation`, also `validation` when its block did not parse | Routines, sessionator |
| `artifact_upload_url` | id, kind (as `put_artifact`); optional commitSha, runId, agentSetVersion, reviewStats | a single-use URL (valid 5 minutes, bound to the caller and these fields) and its expiry: `POST` the artifact's text to it (`curl --data-binary @file`; UTF-8, at most 1 MiB) and it is stored exactly as `put_artifact` would (same provenance, run check and findings queue), answering with the artifact version. Lets MCP-only agents (routines) upload a file without inlining it. The link is signed with the server's signing secret and spent per server process | Routines, sessionator |
| `mark_ready` | id; runId for routines | updated glob (moves to `pr_open` when GitHub confirms). **Refused while the branch conflicts with its base** (`invalid_transition`): the error names the conflicting files ("This branch conflicts with main in a.ts, b.ts and 2 more files. Merge origin/main, resolve, run the checks, push, then call mark_ready again.") and the PR stays a draft, because a conflicted PR never runs its checks and nothing would tell the run. A branch that is only behind its base but merges cleanly is marked, with a `warning` saying how many commits behind it is. When the host is still working out mergeability slop retries briefly (3 tries, 1 s apart), then allows the call and leaves it to the post-ready conflict check. The board's Mark done (PR ready) (REST `mark-ready`) applies the same check | Routines, sessionator; the board's Mark done (PR ready) on a super uses the same transition (REST `mark-ready`), offered only when the latest implementation record is at the PR head |
| `merge` | id, version; optional continue (a super's Merge and continue, row 31; for a same it is an alias of plain Merge for one release: a same with open rollout steps is merged with Merge and kept open, row 14); optional anyway with a reason (Merge anyway, row 14d: required with `anyway`) | updated glob (row 14: `pr_open` → `merging`; the glob joins the board's merge line and, as its head, slop updates the branch, waits for checks on the new head and squash-merges, as the Merge button does (row 14); with continue, the super returns to `in_progress` once merged) | Sessionator (`slop merge`, `slop merge --continue`); denied to agents in the agent set (with `gh pr merge`, `gh api`, `slop merge` and `slop call merge`); slop can't tell an agent from its developer, since both use the developer's sign-in, so the deny rules are the guard |
| `review_label` | id, version, label (FR, CR, QA), kind (`submit_items`, `save_draft_item`, `remove_draft_item`, `send_items`, `tick`, `resubmit`); items for `submit_items`; text (and itemId to edit a draft) for `save_draft_item`; itemId for `remove_draft_item`; itemId and done for `tick` | updated glob (rows 27–30) | Claude app, sessionator; approving and re-opening a review stay on the board (sign-off is human, like merging) |
| `report_failure` | id, reason; runId for routines; optional agentSetVersion; optional `kind: 'needs_pairing'` and `decision` (the decision the routine would have had to guess) | updated glob; with `needs_pairing` the failure carries `kind` and `decision`, and the glob is never retried by slop | Routines, sessionator |
| `escalate_to_same` | id, reason; optional criterion, evidence, runId | updated glob | Routines, sessionator |
| `get_board` | board | board settings: repo, base branch (the repo's default branch from GitHub, cached), time zone, environments (the dev branch-deploy environments only), enabled integrations (`integrations.deploy`: the deploy provider and its settings, or null) | Agents, sessionator |
| `get_agent_set` | board | the board's agent set (agents, commands, hooks, settings, CLAUDE.md section) with its version, and beside it the board's local-run spec (outside the version) | Sessionator (`sstor init`), routines |
| `get_conventions` | board; optional area | without an area: the knowledge index (each document's title, area, description and audience, meaning the agents that must always be given it) plus approved learnings; with an area: that area's documents | Agents |
| `search_text` | board, query; optional mode (`current` default / `all_time`), from, to (ISO dates), glob, group, sourceTypes | up to 10 cited chunks (source, date, title, link, glob), ranked; replaced and legacy items labelled | Agents, chat |
| `search_semantic` | board, query; same options | the nearest chunks by meaning, cited the same way; an `llm_unavailable` error (503 on HTTP) while the embedding model is down | Agents, chat |
| `ask_board` | board, question; optional globId | an answer written from the board's records and slop's help, with citations (source, title, date, link, glob, status, and the newer choice when replaced); `answered` is false when the answer cites nothing, and then `answer` says what was searched and any text the model still gave, which is not from the records, is returned as `uncitedAnswer`; one-off, nothing stored; `llm_unavailable` (503) while the chat model is busy or down | Agents |
| `search_changes` | board, query or path (a file path); optional from, to, glob, group | change summaries with the reason each was made, newest first, cited | Agents, chat |
| `get_plan` | id; optional version | plan.md with version history | Agents, chat |
| `get_change` | id; optional includeDiff | change summary, files, optionally the diff | Agents, chat |
| `get_build_results` | glob, commit or environment | build failures | Agents, chat |
| `get_test_results` | glob, commit or environment | test runs and failures | Agents, chat |
| `import_knowledge` | board, documents (name, content, optional frontmatter) | imported document ids; admins only | Claude in a session |
| `submit_learning` | board, source glob id, type (gotcha, pattern, agent-behaviour, fact; the `decision` type is removed), statement, evidence; optional suggested target, agentSetVersion, runId, and `document` (name, area, audience, description, content: a whole new-document proposal from `/kb-bootstrap`, for which the source glob is optional) | KB item id (`s1k3`) and `globId` (also `s1k3`: the decision glob that shows it on the board has the item's ID) | Agents |
| `list_kb_items` | board, optional status (open by default) | the board's KB items: id, version, type, statement, evidence, target, draft and its preview, processing state and error, flags (contradicts, possibly covered, stale, duplicate of), and for decided ones who decided and `outcome.via` | Admins |
| `decide_kb_item` | id, version, decision (`approve` or `reject`), reason (required to reject, and to approve a contradicting item), editedDraft (approve only: the full section or value in place of the draft) | the decided item | Admins |
| `get_review_guide` | repo | the board's review guide: its documents with area `review_guide`, for each board on that repo the caller is a member of (as built) | review agents |
| `get_report` | board, period (`YYYY-MM` or `YYYY`) | the board's time report computed now from the event log (only time on the board's globs): board, period, kind, work time zone, when it was computed, and the CSV (developer, period, RnD hours, maintenance hours, % RnD); the board's admins (as built, slice 10) | Admins, Claude app |

**Splitting a glob (`split_glob`, the board's Split, `POST /api/globs/{id}/split`) is a scope cut (s15f144).** A glob is sized for review: one PR, one QA and one sign-off, and parallel work happens inside the run as workstreams (see the size check). A split is for work that could each ship alone: it cuts a glob into **independent globs**, with no parent and no gate between them. `GlobService.split` does it in one transaction, so a half-finished split never reaches the board. Only a glob in Planning that has no branch, run or PR can be split (a started glob is refused with `invalid_input`; a stale version gets `version_conflict`). `parts` has 2–10 entries. Part 0 is the original glob: it keeps its ID, history and choices, gets the new title and a new plan.md version, and is one of the pieces, **signing off on its own** like any glob (no sign-off relation from it to the other parts). The other parts are created in the original's group, with the original's environment, and type and category from the part (default the original's; a sub that the size check made a same splits into subs again). **No gates:** a split writes no relation between the parts and starts none by itself (there is no `autoStart`); a part runs when someone starts it, in parallel with the others. A part may name explicit **needs**: its `after` lists indexes of earlier parts it needs merged first, stored as ordinary start relations (source `person`, or `agent` for `split_glob` over MCP), which hold it like any need (a sub part waits in Planning with `waiting` set and starts when the need merges, as any held sub does). Parts that change the same files are not related: there is no `overlaps` input. A new part also waits for whatever the original waited for (a person's start relations, those not yet merged). Parts go through the same creation rules as `create_glob`, so a sub that waits for an unmerged part is held in Planning, with no branch and no run, until it merges. Merge-policy holds are not read for the new parts. `{part:N}` in any plan becomes part N's glob ID (IDs are allocated first; they can't be predicted, since each category counts on its own). Every part's plan ends with a "Split from <id> on <date>" line (part 0: "Split into …"), kept as provenance. Link attachments are copied to every new part, and text attachments only when the part lists their labels. Every part gets a `GlobSplit` event (source, part, all IDs). With an idempotency key a retry returns the same parts whatever the version (part N is created under the key `split:<key>:<id>:<N>`). The glob view and board list add `split: { source, part, parts }` to a glob that came from a split (`splitsOf` reads the events, so old splits show it too); its card shows "part 2 of 3, split from s15t42" with links. **Old splits (migration 0071):** relations made by earlier splits are kept where they are still needs of globs that exist (source `split`, start gate: they hold like a person's, can be dismissed, and are replaced by an edited `after` list); every open split `sign_off` relation was marked dismissed, so no original is held for its parts any more, and `autoStart` was cleared from the parts (a part in Planning that waited to start by itself stopped waiting and keeps its needs; a flagged glob that was held only for its oversized flag is no longer held). The code that read those relations (the "N/M parts merged" count, `parts` on a holding) is gone.

**`slop` CLI, creating globs:** `slop new [--same|--sub|--super] [--fix] [--routine] [--env <name>] [--after <id>[,<id>...]] <prompt...>` calls `create_glob` (`--feature`, `--task` and `--bug` are hidden aliases; `--feature` means R&D and the glob is stored as a task); `--after` may be repeated or given a comma list, and the output says the glob waits ("waiting for s15t7 to merge, then a routine will implement it"). The REST create and update bodies take `after` too (and create takes intake's `files`), `GET /api/globs/{id}` adds `waitingFor` and `waitedOnBy`, and `POST /api/globs/{id}/actions/start-anyway` is Start now. `sstor --new --after` passes the flag through to `slop new` (in the sessionator repo).

**Investigation artifact (kind `investigation`):** the investigator's plan file, pushed by the orchestrator after Phase 2. It ends with one fenced ```` ```investigation ```` block of JSON: `files` (`path`, `change` of create|modify|delete), `modules`, `expectedLines` (`min`, `max`), `parts` (`name`, `files`, `needs`: names of other parts), `riskTier`, `riskAreas`, `needsHuman` (`needed`, `reason`; a reason is required when needed), `confidence` (low|medium|high), `planHash` (sha256 of plan.md) and `baseCommit`. Slop validates the last such block when the artifact is stored; one that does not validate is stored as text with the reasons in its provenance (`validation`), never refused. The glob view lists it as "Investigation" and shows the parsed fields, or the note. It is listed (not inlined) in `get_context` and indexed as an attachment. **Slop acts on it (s15f52):** when an `investigation` with a valid block is stored (a stale run's is ignored), slop compares it with the glob before the run goes on. A sub whose investigation has `needsHuman.needed`, or lists any `riskAreas`, is escalated to a same through `escalate_to_same`'s transition (row 13a; also allowed in Planning), with the reasons joined as its reason; a same is left alone. When `parts` has at least 2 entries and as many as the board's parts limit (it counts at the limit, not above it: the parts are the investigator's reading of the code, not an estimate), slop raises the oversize flag (a glob in Planning or implementing, unless it was already split) with those reasons and workstreams built from `parts`: ordered so each follows the ones it `needs` (circular needs, or more than 10 parts, give a flag without workstreams), each with its name (the part's title or name), goal, files and plan share (the part's own plan with the plan's context sections it lacks, or the whole original plan to cut from). The flag shows as the Oversized chip in Planning; it holds nothing and the run is never stopped. `get_context` also carries an `investigation` summary (version, run, `validation`, `planHash`, `baseCommit`, the files it expects to touch, risk tier, confidence, `needsHuman`) so an agent can decide reuse without fetching the text. **Investigate-only runs and reuse (s15f53):** a run whose fire text says INVESTIGATE-ONLY works on the base branch of a glob in Planning (row 5a), runs the orchestrator's Phases 1–2, pushes the investigation and stops: no branch, commits, PR, record or `mark_ready`. When the glob later starts, the orchestrator reuses the latest investigation instead of running Phase 2 when its block parsed, its `planHash` equals the hash of the plan it now reads, the base moved little in its relevant files (`git diff --stat <baseCommit> origin/<base> -- <files>`: none deleted or renamed and under about 30 changed lines), and a `needsHuman` it carries has been answered; otherwise it re-investigates, giving the old one to the investigator as a starting point, and the new version supersedes it. Reuse or refresh, and why, is recorded in the implementation record's Decisions.

**Test plan artifact (kind `test_plan`, s15f163):** a second document beside plan.md, versioned the same way (history, provenance, `get_artifact`, `put_artifact(kind: 'test_plan')`, `save_test_plan` / `get_test_plan`, `PUT/GET /api/globs/{id}/test-plan`; a stale `version` is refused with `version_conflict`). Markdown with a matrix (first table headed `Name | Status | Location | Description`, Status `new`, `existing` or `adjusted`, an `adjusted` row saying what changes) and a fenced ```` ```atf-specs ```` block (`specs:` list of repo-relative paths, optional `grep:`). The last block is parsed on save; problems (non-relative or duplicate paths, a matrix location missing from `specs`, an unknown status, a thin `adjusted` description, no block) are stored as a validation note in the artifact's provenance and never refuse the save, and the parsed list is stored on it as `atfSpecs`. Saving one does not run plan.md's overlap, size or dependency checks. `get_context` carries the latest as `testPlan` (full text, version, parsed specs, validation). The glob view has a plan.md / Test plan tab pair (`?section=plan&tab=test-plan`) with the same editor, versions, preview and copy; empty, it offers Write one and Let the investigator draft it. Agents: the investigator reads or drafts it (pushed as v1 for review in Planning), the implementer writes the `new` specs and makes the `adjusted` changes (saving a new version on a move), the change_reviewer treats a `new` or `adjusted` row with no matching spec change, or an `existing` path that is missing, as in-scope, and the tester runs the planned specs it can and records the rest.

**`slop` CLI, uploading artifacts:** `slop put-artifact <id> --kind implementation_plan|postplan|local_review|investigation --file <path|-> [--commit <sha>] [--agent-set <n>] [--review-stats <json>] [--run <runId>]` reads the file (or stdin for `-`) and calls `put_artifact` with its text, so an agent's tool call never carries it. Agents with only MCP (routines) use `artifact_upload_url` and `curl --data-binary @file` instead. Both are preferred to inline `put_artifact` for anything over a few KB.

All tools sit behind OAuth. There is no public MCP endpoint: the Claude app, Claude Code and routines all connect through the OAuth connector.

**REST endpoints (board)**

| Area | Endpoints |
| --- | --- |
| Boards | `GET /boards`, `POST /boards`, `GET /boards/{b}`, `PATCH /boards/{b}/settings`, `GET /boards/{b}/spend` (this month's LLM spend, for any member), `POST /boards/{b}/viewed`, `PUT /boards/{b}/session`, `DELETE /boards/{b}/session` (the person's board bar) |
| Members | `GET /boards/{b}/members`, `POST /boards/{b}/members`, `PATCH /boards/{b}/members/{email}`, `DELETE /boards/{b}/members/{email}` (admins only) |
| Globs | `GET /boards/{b}/globs`, `GET /globs/{id}` (both with each glob's artifact summaries: latest version per kind, version count, commit SHA, provenance, no content), `POST /boards/{b}/globs` (category `action` takes no type and an optional `assignee`; `files` are refused for an action), `PATCH /globs/{id}` (`attributes: { assignee }` edits an open action's assignee), `DELETE /globs/{id}` |
| Intake | `POST /boards/{b}/intake` — processes text and attachments, returns proposed fields (with category confidence and reason, the examples shown and `needsConfirmation`) without saving |
| Size threshold | `GET /boards/{b}/size-threshold` — the learned oversized threshold, the defaults, its bounds and history (members); `POST /boards/{b}/size-threshold/reset` returns it to the defaults (admins; the history keeps a `reset` entry) |
| Type learning | `GET /boards/{b}/type-learning` — `{learned, defaults, corrections}`: the board's latest type corrections not reset, or the catalog defaults (members); `POST /boards/{b}/type-learning/reset` stamps them reset and returns the view (admins; rows stay as history) |
| Intake accuracy | `GET /boards/{b}/intake-accuracy` — how often intake's category and type survived to the merge (members) |
| Actions | `POST /globs/{id}/actions/{start, retrigger, retry-autofix, resolve-conflict, pick-up, take-over, start-again, merge, merge-continue, mark-ready, make-super, done, dismiss}` (`done` takes an optional `note` and `dismiss` a required `reason`, for an action glob only; pick-up and take-over are refused on an action; `make-super` is row 13c; pick-up and take-over take an optional `environment`; `merge-continue` (row 31) and `mark-ready` (Mark done (PR ready)) are for supers and need the latest implementation record at the PR head) |
| Labels | `POST /globs/{id}/labels/{FR, CR, QA}` with version and a command: `submit_items` (items), `save_draft_item` (text, itemId to edit), `remove_draft_item` (itemId), `send_items`, `approve`, `tick` (itemId, done), `resubmit` or `reopen` |
| Artifacts | `GET /globs/{id}/artifacts`, `GET /globs/{id}/artifacts/{kind}?label=` (every version of one artifact, for the viewer), `GET /artifacts/{artifactId}` (presigned URL), `POST /globs/{id}/attachments` (text or link), `POST /globs/{id}/uploads` (presigned upload URL) |
| Signed Off | `GET /boards/{b}/signed-off?cursor=` |
| KB | `GET /boards/{b}/kb` (documents, the agent set's entries with the version the repo holds and the open "Agent set vN" glob, and `catalogUpdates`: documents forked from an older catalog version, with both bodies), `GET /catalog/kb` (catalog entries), `POST /boards/{b}/kb/catalog-imports` (import chosen catalog entries), `POST /boards/{b}/kb/uploads` (upload docs or a folder), `GET /boards/{b}/kb/proposals?status=` (KB items, each open drafted one with its draft's diff against the current target), `GET /boards/{b}/kb/agent-set/file?path=` (one agent-set file: the board's layer to edit, its layer kind, the catalog's text and the served result), `POST /boards/{b}/kb/agent-set/use-catalog` (admins: turn a board file that overrides a catalog file into the catalog file plus board rules) |
| Inbox | `GET /inbox`, `POST /inbox/{meetingId}/attach`, `POST /inbox/{meetingId}/create-glob`, `POST /inbox/{meetingId}/discard` |
| Reports | Computed on demand (as built, slice 10): `GET /boards/{b}/reports` (the work time zone, and `canDownload`, for any member of the board), `GET /boards/{b}/reports/{period}` (JSON with the CSV) and `GET /boards/{b}/reports/{period}/csv` (the download, `slop-report-<board>-<period>.csv`), both for the board's admins; period `2026-09` or `2026` |

**Further write endpoints:** `POST /globs/{id}/actions/merge` (Merge button), `POST /globs/{id}/actions/merge-continue` (Merge and continue, supers and sames with open rollout steps), `POST /globs/{id}/actions/mark-ready` (Mark done (PR ready), supers with the implementation record at the head), `POST /globs/{id}/deploy-now` (any board member; refused while a deploy runs in the glob's environment), `GET /globs/{id}/deploys` (history), `GET /boards/{id}/deploys` (card indicators, the environments with a deploy running, and its ATF indicators (`atf`)), `GET /globs/{id}/tests` (the glob's ATF runs), `POST /globs/{id}/actions/take-over`, `PUT /globs/{id}/plan` (edit plan.md), `POST /kb/{itemId}/approve` (as a learning, as an edit to a document or agent-set file, as the proposed document, or as its draft, optionally edited), `POST /kb/{itemId}/target` (change the target and draft again), `POST /kb/{itemId}/retry` (send a routing- or drafting-failed item back to that stage with fresh attempts) and `POST /kb/{itemId}/reject` (with a reason) (admins). Every write, through REST or MCP, carries the glob or item `version` it read.

**Inbound**

- `POST /webhooks/github`: GitHub App events `push`, `pull_request`, `pull_request_review`, `pull_request_review_comment`, `pull_request_review_thread`, `issue_comment`, `check_run`, `check_suite`; verified with `X-Hub-Signature-256`. Events the adapter doesn't know are ignored, so an App still subscribed to events slop no longer uses is harmless.
- `POST /ingest/communications`: the canonical ingest format, authenticated with the user's ingest secret (Apps Script, pasted text, scripts).
- `POST /webhooks/slack`: the Slack app's shortcut and reaction events, verified with its signing secret.
- `POST /webhooks/aws`: CodeBuild and CodePipeline state changes, and the pipelines' own `slop.ci` events (ATF results), from an EventBridge API destination, authenticated with an API key header.

**Outbound**

- **Routine fire:** POST to the triggerer's routine URL with text naming the glob ID and instructing the agent to call `get_context`.
- **Live hints:** server-sent events on `/boards/{b}/events`, payload `{ kind: "glob.changed" | "glob.deleted" | "board.changed", globId, version }`, or `{ kind: "glob.artifacts", boardId, globId }` (no version; refetched regardless) when an artifact is added, or `{ kind: "glob.deploys", boardId, globId }` (no version) when one of a glob's deploys changes (the board refetches its deploy indicators and the glob's deploy history), or `{ kind: "glob.findings", boardId, globId }` (no version; refetched regardless) when a glob's review sources or findings change (the glob view refetches its findings on it and on every reconnect), or `{ kind: "board.kb", boardId }` after any change to the board's KB items, documents or agent-set files (the Knowledge page refetches on it, on `board.changed` and on every reconnect; `board.changed` is sent too only when the board's agent-set version moves).
- **GitHub App:** create and delete branches, update a branch (rebase), squash merge with title `<id>: <title>` pinned to the checked head's `sha`, create each glob's branch with an empty first commit and open its draft PR with labels, compare commits, read check runs. CodeBuild: StartBuild for glob-branch deploys through the Deployer port.
- **Bedrock:** intake inference, meeting classification, embeddings and board chat through the LLM port.

**Implementation tool contracts**

- **Routine (sub, same):** call `get_context`; check out the glob's branch from origin (cloud sessions start on the default branch) and push only to it; `put_artifact` the implementation plan with its amendments section; call `mark_ready` (slop marks the glob's draft PR ready through its GitHub App, so the routine needs no `gh`); call `report_failure` if it cannot finish. It passes its run ID with every artifact and failure, adds Slop-Run: \<runId> and Slop-Agent-Set: \<version> trailers to every commit, and checks the glob through the MCP before each push.
- **Sessionator (super):** `whoami`, then `create_glob` (type super) and check out the returned branch. On each push to the feature branch, `put_artifact` the implementation record (kind `implementation_plan`) with the commit SHA. After local review, `put_artifact` the local review. Mark the PR ready for review in GitHub (no slop call). Submit learnings with `submit_learning`.

## Implementation and agents

Subs and sames are implemented by Claude Code routines owned by whoever triggered them; supers are implemented by the developer through sessionator or an equivalent tool.

**Routines**

- Each developer creates one routine in their own claude.ai account, used for both subs and sames, from a shared prompt and command. The prompt stays thin and identical: fetch the glob from the slop MCP and follow its instructions using the agents in `.claude/`.
- Each developer stores the routine's fire URL and token in Secrets Manager at `slop/prod/routines/<userId>/default`; slop builds the path from the triggerer. If the triggerer has no routine, slop falls back to the board's default routine owner.
- A routine's cloud environment has a fixed set of repositories, so a developer can also store one routine per board (`slop/prod/routines/<userId>/<boardId>`, as Secrets Manager names can't hold `#`; locally `<email>#<boardId>` in `.routines.json`), used before their default. Either way the routine's environment must include the board's repository, with the Claude GitHub App installed on it. The fire text names the board's repository, and a session that doesn't have it, or can't push to it, reports a failure instead of working in another repository.
- The triggerer is the person whose session created the glob, where slop can determine it.
- Usage, the daily run cap, commits and PRs belong to the routine owner; time follows the planner until a human picks the glob up (slop stores both). Only the routine owner can teleport into a run.
- Sessionator's sstor --routine-setup command handles routine setup and storing the secret.

* **Seats:** every Team seat includes Claude Code according to Anthropic's help center; Premium seats add higher usage. Standard-seat users can own routines but will hit limits sooner.

**Agent system**

- **The agent set is board knowledge.** Each board's agent definitions, commands, hooks and Claude settings are KB items (kinds `agent`, `command`, `hook`, `settings`, `mcp`, `claude_md`), versioned and changed through approved proposals like any other document. The board's **agent-set version** increases with every approved change to any of them, and whenever the catalog's agent set changes.
- **Catalog origin, layered:** a board's agent set is slop's generic agent set (`catalog/agents/` in the public repo) with the board's own layer on top, so catalog changes reach every board without anyone merging. A board row is either an **overlay** on a catalog file, kept separately and appended under `## Board rules` (markdown files) or deep-merged as JSON (`settings.json`: objects merge, arrays concatenate without duplicates, board values win), or a whole **file** the board owns (one it added, or a legacy full copy, which the Knowledge page flags as overriding the catalog and an admin can turn back into the catalog file plus board rules). Hooks and `mcp.json` take no overlay. A catalog file with no board row is served as is; an overlay whose catalog file is gone is kept but not served. Slop hashes the catalog's agent set at start and bumps the agent-set version of every board that followed a different hash; approved learnings write only the overlay, and a learning that should change the generic text is a catalog candidate, i.e. a suggested change to `catalog/`.
- **Delivery:** `sstor init` fetches the board's agent set with `get_agent_set(board)` with sstor's own sign-in (the developer's OAuth login through sstor's Cognito app client), writes it into the checkout and records the version in `.claude/slop-agent-set.json`. The files are committed in the project repo, so every checkout, including a routine's cloud checkout, has the agents registered from the start. **New versions reach the repo through slop, not through runs:** every bump of the board's agent-set version (an approved agent-file change, a catalog change) queues one `commit_agent_set` outbox job per board (default lane, board-wide: `globId` is `board-<id>`, not generation-checked) and pushes its `runAfter` to ten minutes after the bump, so a batch of approvals makes one commit. When it runs, slop opens the board's "Agent set vN" glob, or retitles the one still open (never more than one open per board; creation key `agent-set:<board>:<n>`): a same in Doing, picked up by slop itself, so its branch and draft PR are provisioned as usual and no routine run starts. The job then commits, as the GitHub App through the code host port (`commitToBranch`: the Git data API, one commit on the branch's head, never forced), the files `get_agent_set` serves that `sstor init` writes under `.claude/` (agents, commands, hooks; hooks executable) and `.claude/slop-agent-set.json`, removes the files the branch's previous manifest listed that the set no longer has, and ends the message with the trailer `Slop-Agent-Set: N`. Settings, `.mcp.json` and CLAUDE.md are merged into a checkout by `sstor init` and aren't committed. The job waits (no attempt spent) until the glob's branch exists, leaves a branch whose manifest already says version N alone, and does nothing when the base branch already holds the board's version and no such glob is open. The PR shows the diff from the committed version; merging it brings the repo, and so routines and checkouts, to that version. If the version moves again before it merges, the newer set is committed to the same branch; once it merges, a later bump opens the next glob. A run never writes agent-set files: the orchestrator compares `get_agent_set`'s version with `.claude/slop-agent-set.json`, carries on with the committed set when they differ and records both versions (the `Assumptions` attachment in a routine, the session output locally). The Knowledge page shows the committed version (`committedVersion`, read from the base branch; null when unknown) and the open glob (`openGlobId`) beside the board's version.
- **Local-run spec:** what sstor builds and launches in a session's server window is one board knowledge row of its own kind (`local_run`, name `local-run`): JSON `{ "build"?: string, "launch": string }`, each one shell command line (no newlines or NUL, at most 2,000 characters), no other keys. sstor runs it on developers' machines, so it changes only through an approved KB item (a learning routed or retargeted to it is drafted as the whole value; the first value arrives the same way, and there is no import or upload of it), is checked on every write and every read (a stored value that fails the check isn't served, and the problem is reported), and is stored in canonical form (build first, two-space JSON) so its versions diff cleanly. It is not part of the agent set: changing it bumps no agent-set version, it is never overlaid or written under `.claude/`, and its effect checks go by time. `get_agent_set` and the download carry it beside the files (`localRun`, null when the board has none); `sstor init` writes it to the gitignored `.sstor/local-run.json`, removes that file when the board has none, and leaves it alone when slop sends no value (an older server) or reports the stored one invalid. The Knowledge page shows it read-only.
- **Routines** use the committed copy. At the start of an unattended run the orchestrator compares the committed version with `get_agent_set`; if slop's is newer it writes the new files and commits them with the glob, and they take effect from the next session (files written during a session only register in the next one). Routines need no session-start hook.
- **What goes in a definition and what goes in a document:** short rules that always apply to one agent go in that agent's definition, whether generic or project-specific (they are always present and carry system-prompt weight). Bulky or situational knowledge (conventions, architecture, testing guides) goes in documents, loaded by area and passed to agents by their audience.
- The knowledge base is searchable at runtime through MCP tools such as `get_conventions`, `search_text` and `search_semantic`, and agents submit learnings with `submit_learning`.
- **KB catalog:** generic, non-sensitive starter documents in slop's public repo under `catalog/kb/` (to start: TypeScript conventions, Python conventions, a generic review checklist and a build-doc template), each with the same frontmatter as any KB document (`area`, `audience`, `description`) plus a catalog ID and version. When a board is created, an admin picks the entries to import, with suggestions pre-ticked from the repo's languages (GitHub's languages API). Importing **forks** the entry: the board gets its own versioned copy, records its source (`catalog:typescript_conventions@3`) and can edit it freely. Forks don't sync: when a catalog entry's version moves past the one a board document was forked from (its source still `catalog:<id>@<v>`), the Knowledge page marks the document "Catalog has a newer version" and shows a read-only two-way line diff (board body against catalog body, frontmatter stripped), so an admin can copy changes across by hand; nothing is overwritten.
- **Other ways in:** a project's existing docs are uploaded on the board's KB page (files or a folder; frontmatter sets area and audience, otherwise Haiku suggests them and the admin confirms), or imported from a session with the admin-only `import_knowledge` MCP tool. Knowledge only the code holds is drafted by the `/kb-bootstrap` command, which runs in a checkout, reads the repo and submits the missing documents (build doc, conventions as practised, architecture overview) as new-document proposals. All three paths end as KB documents or proposals, so admin approval still governs what agents receive.
- **Core documents:** every board should have a build document, conventions and an architecture overview (areas `build`, `conventions`, `architecture`). A board is missing one when no knowledge document has that area. The Knowledge page and the readiness checklist (Settings, Board and repository) say so, with the fix (run `/kb-bootstrap <board>` in a checkout), until a document with that area exists. Weekly mining also raises one `pattern` learning per missing area (signal `missing_doc:<area>`) once the board's oldest glob is at least 7 days old and it has at least 5 merged globs; it raises it again only if the earlier one was rejected and the area is still missing 60 days later.
- **Script catalog:** slop keeps reusable scripts and config templates; boards select the ones they use, and builds and setup fetch them from slop's public endpoint. Claude Code generates the initial set during the build: a CodeBuild buildspec for PR checks that reads the glob labels; a buildspec that deploys a branch to the glob's environment; an ATF trigger buildspec; the sub gate GitHub Action; a `review-guide.md` template; a PR-Agent configuration template; and the Google Meet Apps Script. The `sstor` changes are specified in the sessionator document.

**Self-improvement (KB items)**

Each proposal is an `s<board>k<n>` KB item linked to the globs that produced it, and nothing reaches the knowledge base or the agent set without human approval.

- **Provenance:** every run, artifact, failure report and learning records the board's agent-set version it ran with (from `.claude/slop-agent-set.json`), alongside the model. Slop can then compare signals before and after any change. Runs take their version from their commits: the catalog orchestrator and `/finalise` add a `Slop-Agent-Set: <version>` trailer to every commit, recorded on the push's `CommitPushed` event; a glob without one takes the highest version on its artifacts, failures or learnings, else its version is unknown (and it is left out of agent-file effect checks). An approved agent-file change records the board's agent-set version it created, which is where its effect check starts. A superseded run's `RunEnded` event records its cause (take-over, start again, pick-up, PR closed, deleted).
- **Capture (self-reports):** at the end of each run (orchestrator phase 6 for sames and subs, `/finalise` for supers, the end of each routine run) the orchestrator calls `submit_learning` once per learning with type (gotcha, pattern, agent-behaviour, fact), statement, evidence (glob, files, review findings or failures) and an optional suggested target. A choice made in the work is not a learning: the agent records it in its implementation record's `## Decisions`, where slop extracts it as a choice. `agent-behaviour` learnings report something an instruction would have prevented: a review finding the implementer should never have produced, a test pass that failed because of how the code was written, and above all a developer correcting the agent in the session. `fact` learnings record how the system is now: the orchestrator submits one per architecture or operations change (new or moved modules, tables, services, flows, deploy steps), routed to the matching document and drafted to replace the outdated description. The change_reviewer and the independent reviewer also check that a behaviour change describes itself in the spec, guide or catalog README, and raise a missing or stale update as an in-scope finding. Agents never edit the knowledge base or the agent set directly.
- **Signals slop mines (weekly job, marked as mined):** review findings are classified by Haiku as they arrive (e.g. "missing test for changed function", "unhandled error path") so they can be counted. Each local review (`put_artifact` with `local_review`) is queued as a review source in the same transaction; nothing is parsed or asked in the request. A background job (the KB pipeline's claim, lease, deadline, retry and AI-unavailable rules, with a 90-second deadline per split call and a 30-second one per classification; a blank review has nothing to split and makes no call) splits each source into findings: a local review that uses the change_reviewer's IN-SCOPE and SUGGESTIONS sections is split by those sections' list items (with the round and the `[path:line]` they name; status tables and sections compiling earlier items are skipped), any other review by one Haiku call whose findings are kept only when their quote is found verbatim in the review. A finding repeated in a later version or round of the glob's reviews is stored once when its text is the same (exact-normalised-text dedupe, by a fingerprint of its text); Haiku splits of different versions paraphrase, so their findings mostly don't collapse. Each finding is then classified by one Haiku call into a fixed class list (missing-test, weak-test, unhandled-error, edge-case, concurrency, security, input-validation, timeout-resource, performance, logic-error, ui-state, migration-schema, spec-drift, convention, dead-code-comments, dependency, scope, or other with a note saying what the class would be); a finding still unclassified after three attempts shows as "couldn't classify" and counts toward nothing. The glob view lists the findings by class with their counts, severity, location, round, source and commit, as plain text.
  The mining job runs per board when 7 days have passed since its last run (checked hourly; a `board_jobs` lease keeps two servers from both running it, a run records its result only while it still holds the lease, and each run's transaction also takes the board job's lock, so a run that outlives its lease can't raise the same signal twice), and admins can Run now from the Knowledge page: it takes the lease and answers at once (already running when another run holds it), and the header shows the job running (polled slowly while it runs) and then the last run's figures. Code-host calls that read manifests time out after 30 seconds. Each run measures the last 28 days of the board's events, artifacts and findings (findings by when their review or comment was written, not when it was split); a signal over its threshold becomes an open `mined` KB item (submitted by `slop`, the board's current agent-set version) that the KB pipeline routes, deduplicates and drafts like a submitted one. Its statement comes from the signal's template, its evidence gives the window, figures (occurrences only when they differ from the affected count), threshold, affected globs and up to 5 quoted examples (a superseded run's cause, how long it had run and the last reason recorded; a failed commit's failing check when the build event names it, or a note that it wasn't compared with the base branch and may be inherited from a red base), its suggested target is the agent file (or document) named below, and it carries the signal (key, figures, up to 20 globs, examples), shown on the card as a figures table with a "Mined" badge. Signals, with their data and thresholds (`min` globs unless stated):
  - Recurring classes of local-review findings (`finding:<class>`; IN-SCOPE findings, `other` left out): the implementer isn't preventing them. At least 3 globs and 25% of reviewed globs.
  - Many deviations from the plan in the implementation record (`plan_amended`; 3 or more lines under the latest record's `## Deviations`, for every glob type), or plan.md edited before a rerun (`plan_edited_rerun`; the summary, plan.md or an attachment changed between a failed or superseded run and the next queued one). plan.md v1, whether written at creation or backfilled from the summary (provenance `backfill`), is the baseline and never counts as an edit, so `plan_edited_rerun` counts fall for globs whose v1 was first written after a run ended. At least 3 globs and 25% of globs with a record; at least 2 globs.
  - Review cycles reaching the round cap (`review_cap`; supers left out, since a super keeps one review with rounds per strand; the local review's `reviewStats` when recorded, with a cap of at least 2, else 3 or more rounds named in the review's `##` headings), and tester FAIL→fix loops (`tester_loops`; from `reviewStats`): the implementer or tester. At least 3 globs and 30% of reviewed globs (of globs with review stats for loops).
  - Recurring `report_failure` reasons (`failure:<reason>`; run failures and interactive failures, the reason lower-cased without URLs, IDs, SHAs and numbers, first 80 characters), and routine runs ending in take-over or start-again (`run_superseded`; the recorded cause, or for older runs the events of the same transition): usually an orchestrator gap, especially in unattended mode. At least 3 failures with one reason; at least 3 runs and 20% of ended routine runs.
  - Recurring workstream patterns (`workstream_pattern`, s15f156; read from each fanned-out glob's latest `WorkstreamsRecorded` event): the same file conflicting when workstreams were integrated (key `workstream_pattern:conflict:<path>`), or a workstream of the same name ending `failed` (key `workstream_pattern:failed:<name>`), in at least 2 fanned-out globs of the window. The rate is over fanned-out globs; the learning targets `risk_and_size` (how to cut around the file, when work should not be its own workstream). The orchestrator also submits an `agent-behaviour` learning after a fanned-out run whose cut was wrong.
  - Frequent sub-to-same conversions (`sub_converted`; a failed sub gate), and CI failing after local checks passed (`ci_after_local`; distinct failed commits per glob that had a local review or a routine's push, leaving out failures marked as inherited from a red base branch): local checks or scope control not matching the project. At least 3 and 30% of gated subs; at least 2 globs and 10% of globs with builds.
  - Independent-review findings people say were wrong (`review_false_positive:<category>`): no agent (a review-guide document item, type pattern). Feedback is a person's `/wrong` (or "false positive") reply on a finding's inline comment, with the thread resolved or not, or a thumbs-down reaction on it; a plain resolve with no such reply is stored as `resolved` and not counted. At least 3 globs with the same category marked wrong, of globs whose independent review in the window had findings. See "Independent review: feedback" under Publishing.
  - New libraries or frameworks with no conventions yet (`dependency:<name>`; dependencies a merged glob's commit added to a `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements*.txt`, `go.mod` or `Cargo.toml`, read from the code host, that no board document mentions): a missing document. All found in one run are raised as one item, and each name only once.

  - Choices that keep mattering (`choice_in_use:<choice id>`; s15f67): no agent (a `pattern` learning, suggested target the document area closest to the choice, which routing decides as usual). Choices are the automatic record and nobody sees them on the board until they prove useful: usage is counted (no model calls) each time a choice is returned to an agent or to chat, in `choice_usage` (one counter row per board, choice, day and glob, `''` when the retrieval named none; pruned after 90 days by the mining run). A return is a choice among the `related` hits of `get_context` (counted under the glob the bundle was for; the glob's own `decisions` field is not counted, nor is a choice for the glob it was taken on), among the hits of `search_text` and `search_semantic` (under the `glob` filter when one was given), or among an answer's chat citations (under the glob the question was scoped to). The signal raises a learning for a current (not replaced) choice returned at least 5 times on at least 3 different globs in the last 30 days that no board document covers (the choice's quote or statement does not appear in a document's text, whitespace and case ignored), when it comes from an implementation record, a plan or an attachment; a choice from pasted discussion (the inbox) needs at least 10 returns, and a choice from an accepted learning never counts (it is knowledge already). The learning states the choice and why it matters ("used by s15tA, s15tB, s15tC"), with the quote, its source and its usage as evidence. A choice is raised once: while its learning is open it is refreshed like any signal; if it is rejected it is never raised again for that choice (no 12-week quiet period), and if it is approved the document covers it and the signal stops. Effect checks don't measure it. The glob view's Choices list shows "used N times on M globs" (last 30 days) on a choice returned at all.
  - Repeated corrections of what is key (`knowledge_page:<area>:<verdict>`; s15f63): no agent (a `pattern` learning, suggested target the `knowledge-page-guide` document). 3 or more Pin / Not key verdicts in the last 30 days the same way (`pin` or `hide`) about decisions with something in common: the area of the page entry behind the decision, or, when it is behind none, the decision's scope (`scope <label>`). Verdicts are counted as they stand (an undone one is gone). The evidence quotes up to 5 verdicts with the decision's title. It is raised like any signal (quiet for 12 weeks after a rejection) and is not offered under Watch signal, since no run's outcome measures it.

  A `kb_signals` row per board and signal key keeps a signal from being raised every week. While its item (or the open item it was merged into) is open, each run refreshes the item's figures and adds a "Week of <date>: affected/eligible" line to its evidence ("Mined by slop, week of …" on a submitted item), without drafting it again; a second run on the same day with the same figures adds nothing. Once approved or covered it is never raised again (the effect check watches it). Once rejected or suppressed it stays quiet for 12 weeks, and is raised again after that only at 1.5× the signal's own rate at the time (dedupe may still suppress it). A chain of merged or covered items longer than 10 links counts as no item, so the signal is raised again. A signal below its threshold, or not measured, counts the runs in a row it has been below (an unmeasured one keeps its last figures and measurement time).
