- **MCP access:** slop's MCP is a claude.ai custom connector with Cognito as the OAuth server (see Auth). The Claude app and routines act as the signed-in person with their board role; Claude Code and sessionator log in the same way.
- No notifications outside the app for now.

## Planning inputs and intake

- **Attach** through the MCP as inline text or links; on the board as text, links or file uploads. Links (Google Drive, Slack) are fetched by a `SourceFetcher` port and stored as snapshots. Files are only uploaded through the board.
- **Meeting inbox:** every meeting arrives through its source's adapter and is stored once. An LLM suggests attaching it to existing globs, across boards, or creating new globs from its action items; a person confirms. One meeting can attach to several globs. No automatic attachment to start with. Meetings come from Google Meet notes.
- **Related meetings:** intake searches slop's stored meetings by keyword (semantic search later), not the meeting tools directly.
- **Artifact history:** S3 bucket versioning, with a current-version pointer on the glob. No git.
- **LLM:** Amazon Bedrock via the Converse API, called with slop's IAM role behind the LLM port; prompts live in the core. Claude Haiku 4.5 for intake and meeting classification to start (and for the generated knowledge page, `KNOWLEDGE_PAGE_MODEL`); Opus 5.5 for the KB pipeline. Credential and access failures (an expired AWS sign-in, missing Bedrock permission or model access, a model that isn't found, enabled or valid, no credentials) are a typed *LLM unavailable* condition, not a generic error. The fix names what to do: `aws sso login` with the profile when `AWS_PROFILE` is set, otherwise the server's IAM role; for a model problem, its setting (`INTAKE_MODEL`, `KB_ROUTE_MODEL`, `KB_DRAFT_MODEL`, `CHAT_MODEL`) and model access in the Bedrock console. A model ARN's account ID is masked. Slop tracks health per model and logs each change; `GET /api/health` (members of any board) returns the worst state: ok, down with a plain-language reason and fix, or unknown, with no profile, region or model fields. Intake answers `llm_unavailable` (HTTP 503) with the fix. Throttling, overload, a model not ready, quota bursts, server errors and SDK timeouts are a separate *Bedrock busy* condition (`LlmBusy`): callers wait and retry with backoff, it never marks a model down or raises the AI-paused banner, and the Bedrock clients use adaptive retry with 5 attempts; ten busy answers from one model within ten minutes raise a warning ("Bedrock is throttling <model>") that the next success clears. Other model errors are ordinary failures.
- **Intake inference** returns name, summary, type, `typeReason`, category, group (existing preferred), an environment the input names (one of the board's branch-deploy environments, matched as a word) and `autoTrigger` with a reason, validated against the matrix with optimistic defaults (an unclear or invalid type is a sub). The prompt carries the board's risk areas (path patterns and why), its learned sub size and the examples, and asks the model to list the files it expects to change, since the patterns are matched against them. Through the MCP, explicit fields win. Sames auto-trigger only on an explicit instruction in the input.
  - *Type rules (when a person is needed):* **sub** (the default) is a settled plan with no open questions that one routine can implement and merge, whatever its area; **same** is a settled plan whose result a person must review afterwards (a risk area, user-facing or sensitive work, or a request for review); **super** is a plan with decisions only a person can make during the work (a design to agree, options to choose, "discuss with", open questions, a decisions section with no tasks), where Claude would otherwise guess. Size is separate: big settled work stays one glob sized for one review (the agents divide it into workstreams in the run), and a super is for unsettled work that needs pairing, not for big work. A split is a scope cut: it means intake bundled independent features, and the intake learning records it as that correction. When unsure, sub rather than same; super only on real evidence of an open decision. `typeReason` is the model's one-line reason (null when the type was explicit); the create form shows it under the type. Every super is created parked in Planning for a developer to pick up to pair. `INTAKE_PROMPT_VERSION` is 7.
  - *Type corrections (as built, s15f58):* every change of `type` a person makes through `GlobService` (the glob view's type buttons, `PATCH /globs/{id}`, MCP `update_glob`: sub to same, same to sub, same to super, super to same, sub to super, super to sub) and every accepted *Set type to super* is recorded in `type_corrections` (migration 0043: id, board, glob, from and to type, actor email, time, `signals` jsonb, nullable `reset_at`), in its own transaction after the change: a recording failure is logged and never fails the change. `signals` come from plan.md v1 (`extractTypeSignals`, pure): `openQuestions` (lines under a questions heading, plus lines saying TBD, undecided or unresolved), `decisionWording` ("discuss with", "decide whether", a decisions or options heading), `riskAreaFiles` (the files the plan names that fall under one of the board's risk areas, at most 5) and `sizeTasks` (task lines, null with none). `propose` puts the board's latest 8 corrections not reset into the prompt as examples, newest first and each line clipped ("Changed from same to super: 2 open questions, decision wording, no tasks"), to be followed over the model's own reading when the plan looks similar. A board with none gets the catalog defaults (a settled small change is a sub; a risk-area change a same; a plan with open design questions, "discuss with" or a decisions section and no tasks a super). Board settings shows learned or default with the recent corrections; an admin's reset stamps `reset_at` on the live rows, so they leave the prompt but stay as history, and later corrections teach again. A person's de-escalation (row 13b) is not a type correction (the escalation learning records it).
- **Learned task categorisation (as built, s15f29):** intake learns from how its own choices fared.
  - *Snapshot at create:* every glob gets an `intake_snapshots` row written in the create transaction (never updated, except to fill a missing embedding; a plan rewritten before the glob starts would add version + 1, not built yet): the request as intake read it (the summary when intake didn't run), title, summary, plan.md v1, creator, source (`mcp`, `web`, `api`, or `backfill`), the type, category, group and environment chosen at creation (after the person's edits), intake's category confidence (`high`, `medium`, `low`) and reason, the model, the prompt version (`INTAKE_PROMPT_VERSION`, 3: v1 was the prompt without examples, v2 added them, v3 broadened the sub definition), the globs shown as examples, plan features (`extractPlanFeatures`: tasks, "Done when" lines, untasked sections, areas, paths, words) and the request's embedding. The embedding is computed before the transaction (a model call never runs inside one); if the embedder is unavailable the row is stored without and the job fills it.
  - *Outcome at merge:* `glob_outcomes` holds one row per merged glob (`collectOutcome`, pure): a person's first change of category or type after creation (the gate's own sub to same conversion is recorded as such, with its cause and changed lines, not as a correction), a split, review rounds and test-fail rounds from the local review's stats, findings by severity and class, the glob's own failed builds (builds inherited from a red base are not counted), calendar hours from creation to merge, and the bug globs created within 14 days that name it. It is written at the merge and refreshed once 14 days after it (`final`). Not recorded: working hours, comment counts and routine restarts.
  - *Job:* the `intake_outcome` board job (hourly, with the other learning jobs; Run now in the Intake panel of "What slop has learned") backfills snapshots for globs that have none (rebuilt from plan.md v1 or the summary and the `GlobCreated` event, `backfilled`, with no intake fields, so they serve as examples but not as accuracy), embeds snapshots without a vector, and records or refreshes outcomes.
  - *Use at intake:* `propose` embeds the request and takes the nearest snapshots (cosine, the nearest 15 considered): those a person corrected come first, then the rest, at most 5, shown in the prompt with what they ended up as and what changed ("category changed feature -> bug", "sub converted to same for size, 2400 lines"). The model also returns `categoryConfidence` and `categoryReason`. Retrieval or embedding failure degrades to the prompt without examples; it never fails intake.
  - *Card:* the proposal carries the examples, confidence, reason and `needsConfirmation` (confidence `low`, or the 3 nearest examples disagree about the category; never when the category was explicit). The create form shows "Based on similar globs" and, when confirmation is needed, a notice with a "Category is right" button; the category control is the existing one. The form sends the intake record back with the create so the snapshot keeps it. A confirmation changes nothing stored.
  - *Accuracy:* `GET /api/boards/{b}/intake-accuracy` (members) returns, over merged globs with an intake decision, the share whose category or type was changed, by creation month and by prompt version, and the median changed lines, calendar hours and review rounds by final type and category. The Intake panel of "What slop has learned" shows it beside the types intake learned.
- **Learned size check (as built, s15f28):** intake also judges whether a glob is one PR's worth of work; the rules are under Sub review. `propose` returns `oversized` (the reasons the plan's text gives; the text alone cannot show parts running in parallel, so it is always empty now) and the real check is the model's. The real check is made by `SizeCheckService` when the glob is created (`GlobService.create`, before the transaction, since it calls the intake model) and when plan.md is saved in Planning (`ArtifactService`, after the save and not awaited). It is in the core behind the `SizeAssessor` port, so `GlobService` and `ArtifactService` work without it. Tables: `glob_size_checks`, `size_thresholds`, `size_threshold_changes` (migration 0033). A new glob made by a split has no size check of its own until its plan is judged, so the learning ignores it. Since s15f50 the check also carries a code-based forecast, a reference class and a learned calibration (migration 0043; see Size forecast under Sub review): `SizeCheckService` takes the code view and the embedder, and `get_context`'s `size` carries the forecast.
- **Create on the board:** a free-text box with attachments and a Process button; the LLM fills a structured form (name, summary, actor and mode, group, auto-trigger; the fix flag and work kind appear as inferred chips the person can flip, and there is no category picker) that the user checks and edits before Create. The form can also be filled directly.

* **Meeting sources:** Google Meet notes via Apps Script. Each person's script uses their own ingest secret. Meetings arrive without a board, in a shared inbox every slop user can see; once attached to a glob they also appear on that glob's board. Attachment is always confirmed by a person. The inbox has a discard action for meetings that don't belong.
* **Other sources** (Slack threads, pasted text, legacy docs) post the canonical ingest format; see Knowledge and context.

## Knowledge and context

Slop is a context manager: it holds what is known about the work, decides what is current and relevant, and serves curated context to people and agents.

**Knowledge sources**

| Source | Stored | Chunking | Best queried by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Meetings | Gemini notes; transcripts too for formulation meetings | Notes by section; transcripts in \~500-token windows | Text and semantic search, by date and participants |
| Slack threads | The thread with author names kept in every window, plus an LLM summary of its conclusion and each participant's position | Summary as one chunk, plus windows of consecutive messages | Text and semantic search |
| plan.md and implementation records | Every version; older versions marked superseded | By heading | `get_plan` with history; search |
| Code changes | Per merged glob: commit SHA, PR link, changed file paths and an LLM summary of what changed and why; full diff fetched from GitHub on demand | One chunk | `search_changes` (summaries plus trigram match on paths, by date); agents also use `git log -S` / `-G` |
| Build failures | Only the error messages (the first piece is recorded on the glob's head checks, see Merging) and what failed, written by an LLM from the tail of the failing build log and the test report | One chunk plus structured rows | `get_build_results` |
| Test runs | Structured rows: glob, commit, environment, counts, failing test names and messages | Not chunked | `get_test_results` |
| Legacy Jira issues | Description, comments, linked commits and a "why this changed" summary | One chunk, split if long | Search (legacy tier) |
| Legacy Google Docs | The document | By heading | Search (legacy tier) |
| Choices | Their own items (source type `decision`, kept for compatibility), linked to their source | One chunk | Search; shown on globs |
| Knowledge base | Lives in slop, versioned in Postgres, per board. Each document has an area and an audience (the agents that must always be given it). Served only through MCP tools; agents save what they fetch under `.reviews/` for the session. Slop indexes it directly | By heading | `get_conventions`; search |

**Knowledge delivery:** slop is the source of truth. `sstor init <board>` runs before Claude starts whenever sessionator creates or syncs an instance: it fetches the board's agent set with `get_agent_set`, using sstor's own sign-in, and writes it into the checkout, where it is committed (see Agent system). On a first install it writes the slop server entry into `.mcp.json` from sstor's own configuration (slop's URL and the Claude Code client ID, neither sensitive), so Claude Code can authenticate. Routines use the committed agent set; a newer one reaches the repo through the "Agent set vN" glob slop commits (see Agent system). The board ID comes from an argument, an environment variable, or `.sstor/sstor.conf` (`SLOP_BOARD`). If slop is unreachable, the committed copy is used with a warning. All other board knowledge (build commands, conventions, architecture, decisions, history) and the board's settings are fetched at run time through the MCP tools (`get_board`, `get_context`, `get_conventions`, the search tools), authenticated by the connector's OAuth. Existing project docs (e.g. a project's `.sstor/docs/`) are imported into the board's knowledge base once, then removed from the project repo. Review guides are also served from slop rather than the repo (see Review and testing). Details are in the sessionator document.

- **Card search and content search are separate.** Board filters find globs by structured fields; the chat and agent tools search content. The chat can use both.
- **The current codebase is not indexed.** Agents with a checkout use grep and git; slop holds the history of changes, not the code. GitHub's own MCP server can complement slop's tools for code and PR search.
- **Raw diffs are not indexed.** Change summaries are generated with one cheap LLM call per merged glob.

**Order of authority** when sources disagree: merged code and the implementation record, then current choices (newest first), then plan.md, then older discussion. Replaced choices are history, not instructions. A choice is a later, explicit decision made in the work, so it outranks the plan it amends. Context bundles state this order (`authority`) and carry the glob's own choices (the `decisions` field, name kept).

**Choices and replacement**

- On ingest, an LLM extracts choices from each communication (statement, topic, date, source) and compares them with existing choices on the same topic.
- A contradicting newer choice marks the older one `replaced` (stored as `superseded`, with `superseded_by`, for compatibility), linked to its replacement. Replaced items are not hidden: they are heavily down-ranked and labelled "replaced by…" with a one-line reason, so readers and the LLM see both the history and what is current. Replacement is judged by an LLM comparing a new choice with the most similar active choices on the board; plan versions replace each other automatically. Choices from one source text never replace each other. A replacement is applied only when the model classes the two as the same subject (`sameSubject`), both quotes check out, and either both choices are in one glob or the newer choice's text names the older one's glob; otherwise it is only a hint for a person to confirm.
- Choices appear on the glob with their sources. Members confirm or undo a replacement (as built below); marking a choice wrong is not built yet. A choice is not a decision glob: nobody signs it off.
- Newer material ranks higher when relevance is equal.

**Context assembly**

- A core use case, `assembleContext(glob, purpose, tokenBudget)`, returns a cited bundle: the plan, the implementation record, active choices linked to the glob or its topic, the most relevant recent communication excerpts, related past globs with their change summaries, current test results and relevant conventions.
- It serves routines and sessionator (`get_context`), plan drafting for sames, glob generation from inbox meetings, and the board chat.
- Two modes: slop pushes a curated bundle; agents pull anything further through the search tools.
- **Plan and record in full.** `get_context` always includes plan.md and the glob's implementation record (when one exists) in full, and attachments labelled Clarifications or Assumptions in full (they carry intent that agents must follow and pass verbatim). Every other artifact (local reviews, other attachments) is listed with kind, label, version, commitSha, size and a one-line description, so a revisited glob doesn't flood an agent's context with overlapping documents. `include` (e.g. `['attachment:Notes', 'local_review']`, or `'all'` for everything) returns the named artifacts in full, and `get_artifact(id, kind, label?)` returns one artifact's latest version. The record is the response's `implementationRecord` (version, commitSha, content).
- **One implementation record per glob.** Every glob, of every type, has plan.md (the planner's intent and "Done when") and one implementation record in the same format: Approach, Decisions (each with why, rejected alternatives and trade-offs), Deviations (from plan.md, with reasons), What was built (by area, with commits), Traps and Open items. It is stored as the `implementation_plan` artifact (an agent set may still write `postplan`, an alias for the same record) and kept up to date as work proceeds: started in Phase 2 from the chosen proposal (the investigator's alternatives stay in the local plan file), updated in Phases 3–5, completed and pushed with the HEAD commit in Phase 6, and for supers started at the first agreement and pushed after each push and at finalise. Migration `0026` made each existing glob's latest postplan the next version of its record; where the glob also had a decision log (s15f5), the log is kept below the postplan in the same document, with the older versions as history.

**In the architecture**

- Core modules alongside the board: **Knowledge** (sources, choices, replacement), **Context** (assembly and ranking) and **Generation** (globs and plans), using the existing `SearchIndex` and LLM ports.
- Postgres holds it all: a `knowledge_items` table with `source_type`, `occurred_at`, `created_at`, glob links, `status` and `superseded_by`, and a chunks table with full-text, trigram (`pg_trgm`, for identifiers) and vector indexes.
- Added MCP tools: `get_test_results` (by glob, commit or environment) and `get_change` (summary, files and optionally the diff for a glob).

**Build order**

1. Glob, plan and communication search, plus a basic context bundle ranked by authority and recency.
2. Code change summaries and test result tools.
3. Choice extraction and replacement.

**Search modes:** searches take a mode. **Current** (default) favours recent material, heavily down-ranks replaced items and ranks legacy last. **All time** removes recency weighting and includes replaced and legacy items, labelled with date and status. An optional date range applies to both. The chat switches to all-time mode when a question implies older discussion; the board has an *Include history* toggle; the MCP search tools take the same parameter.

**Chat**

- Conversations, messages, tool calls and citations are stored in Postgres per user per board. Recent turns are sent each time; older turns are summarised.
- Chat answers are never indexed automatically; a *save to knowledge* button lets a person keep one.
- The agent loop is plain code in the container using Anthropic's TypeScript SDK with its Bedrock client, so it can switch to the direct Anthropic API by configuration. AgentCore is not used.

**LLM usage and cost**

| Workload | Model | Expected cost |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Embeddings | Bedrock Titan Text Embeddings V2 | Negligible, including the backfill |
| Ingest processing (choices, change summaries, classification, intake) | Haiku 4.5 | A few dollars a month |
| KB pipeline (routing, dedupe and drafting of learnings) | Opus 5.5 | Cents per learning |
| Chat and context assembly | Sonnet 5.5 for chat as built (`CHAT_MODEL`); Haiku by default for context assembly; Sonnet for plan drafting or on request | Tens of dollars a month, depending on use |

- Prompt caching for the chat's system prompt and tools; a cap on tool steps and retrieved tokens per question; batch inference for the backfill; usage tracked per feature with an AWS Budgets alert.
- No self-hosted LLM. A small open embedding model on CPU in the container is an option if portability ever outweighs quality.
- Search quality levers: structure-aware chunking, a source/title/date/glob header on each chunk, and a reranker (e.g. Cohere Rerank on Bedrock) if needed. ParadeDB's `pg_search` adds BM25 ranking if Postgres full-text ranking proves weak.

**Inputs**

- **Paste:** an *Add to knowledge* box on the board and an `ingest_text` MCP tool, so the Claude app can save a conversation, optionally linked to a glob.
- **Google Meet notes:** the Apps Script polling each person's Drive for Gemini notes and transcripts.
- **Slack:** a small Slack app with a *Send thread to slop* message shortcut (or a `:slop:` reaction); each thread is one communication. Channel-level ingestion can come later. A workspace admin approves the app's installation.
- **Legacy Google Docs:** a one-off Apps Script import, tagged legacy.

**Backfill (legacy tier)**

- Import every issue from the team's previous issue tracker (Jira is the first importer), with descriptions and comments, and the commits linked to each issue, as knowledge items, not globs.
- Generate one "why this changed" summary per issue from its text and linked commits, in batch.
- Skip commits without an issue link; agents find them with `git log`. Skip CodeBuild history.
- Legacy items keep their original dates, rank lowest, are excluded from context bundles unless nothing newer covers the topic, and are never used for choice extraction. The import window is chosen per board (e.g. the last 12–18 months).
- Choice extraction runs only on recent communications (about the last three months).
- All imports go through the canonical ingest endpoint via scripts, oldest first.

**Storage and search configuration** (starting point, to be tuned on real data)

- **Tables:** `knowledge_items`, one row per source item (board, `source_type`, title, `occurred_at`, `created_at`, authority tier, status, `superseded_by`, linked globs, external reference, text or S3 key for large originals); `chunks` (item, position, header, text, full-text, trigram and vector indexes); structured `test_runs`, `test_failures` and `build_failures` tables.
- **Chunk headers** contain only stable facts known at ingest: source type, date, title and section, e.g. `[Meeting · 2026-09-14 · "Sync retry design" · Decisions]`. Missing titles are written by the ingest LLM pass. Glob links are filterable metadata, not header text, so linking never forces re-embedding.
- **Chunk sizes:** about 300–600 tokens; Slack threads kept whole up to about 1,000 tokens.
- **Language:** English full-text configuration. Content in other languages still matches exact words and works in semantic search (Titan V2 is multilingual).
- **Embeddings:** Titan Text Embeddings V2, 1024 dimensions, cosine similarity, HNSW index in pgvector.
- **Keyword search:** Postgres full-text search plus a trigram index for identifiers, error strings and file paths.
- **Ranking (current mode):** recency decay with a 90-day half-life; authority weights; replaced items heavily down-ranked and labelled; legacy demoted. All-time mode removes recency decay.
- **Board search box:** reciprocal rank fusion of keyword and semantic results. **Chat:** Claude chooses among the tools; Cohere Rerank on Bedrock reorders the top 50 to the best 10.
- **Retrieval budget:** at most about 10 chunks or 8,000 tokens per search call.
- **Chat and agent tools:** `search_text`, `search_semantic`, `search_changes`, `get_glob`, `get_plan` (with versions), `get_change`, `get_build_results`, `get_test_results`.
- **Volume:** sized for a small team, around 7 hours of meetings a week; ingest costs stay at a few dollars a month.


**As built (slice 8, search store)**

- **Store:** migration `0023_search_store` creates the `vector` and `pg_trgm` extensions, `knowledge_items` (unique per board and `external_ref`; `body` and `body_key` exist but are unused, text lives in chunks) and `chunks` (generated English `tsv`, `vector(1024)` with an HNSW cosine index, a trigram index on the text, a partial index on chunks still waiting for an embedding). Chunks carry `board_id` too, and every query filters on it.
- **Indexed so far:** glob summaries, the latest plan, implementation record, local review and attachments of each glob, board knowledge documents, approved learnings (not recorded as choices), one change summary per merged commit, and choices (see the choice notes below; they are written by `DecisionPipeline`, not by the indexer). Pasted inbox items (source types `meeting`, `thread`, `doc`; see the inbox notes below) are written by `InboxService`, not the indexer. Imported Jira issues and Google Docs are inbox items indexed at the `legacy` authority tier (they stay legacy once attached, and so do the choices taken from them); Slack has no producer yet. Only the latest version of a plan is indexed.
- **Sync:** the index is derived. `SearchIndexer` compares a content hash per item and rewrites only what changed (the start-up run is the backfill); a board is re-synced about 10 seconds after a change to its material and every 15 minutes regardless, so results lag writes by that long. Embedding runs after the keyword rows exist, so text search works first and semantic coverage fills in. While Bedrock is unavailable, chunks keep a null embedding, nothing fails or counts an attempt, and the job probes once a minute. A merged change is queued searchable by title, then Haiku writes its "why" from the glob's plan and the changed files (at most 150 words; it waits the same way).
- **Authority weights:** merged code and implementation records 1.0, choices 0.95 (a choice weighs what its source weighs: choices from an implementation record's `## Decisions` are indexed at 0.95, those from plan.md, Clarifications and Assumptions at 0.9, and those from pasted meetings, threads and documents at 0.6; migration `0052_choice_authority` re-weighted existing ones), approved plans, knowledge and learnings 0.9, discussion (reviews, attachments) 0.6, legacy 0.3. Score = relevance x authority x recency x status; recency halves every 90 days and replaced items count 0.15, both only in current mode; legacy sorts after everything else in current mode.
- **Chunking:** headings split sections, paragraphs pack to about 450 tokens (300 minimum, 600 maximum), no overlap.
- **Tools and route:** `search_text`, `search_semantic` and `search_changes` and `GET /api/boards/:b/search` (`q`, `history`, `from`, `to`, `glob`, `group`) all check board membership first. The route fuses keyword and semantic results (reciprocal rank fusion) and returns `semantic: "unavailable"` with keyword results when the embedder is down; the board header has the search box with an *Include history* toggle.
- **`get_context` `related`:** the bundle gains `related`: up to five cited results for the glob's title and summary in current mode, without the glob's own items. The semantic arm queries with title and summary and the keyword arm with the title only; with the embedder down or slow (5 seconds) it is keyword-only. Existing fields are unchanged.
- **Not yet:** reranking, the S3 body key, and the other sources above (chat is built: see the next section).

**As built (slice 8, board chat)**

- **What shipped:** a chat panel opened from the board header (beside the search box), and `ChatService` in core. A question runs one retrieval, then one model call. Retrieval is the board search box's (keyword and semantic fused, keyword alone while the embedder is down), in current mode, or all-time with *Include history*, optionally scoped to a glob or a group; choices are indexed items, so they come back ranked by the usual authority, current ahead of replaced. The top results are numbered and sent to the chat model (`CHAT_MODEL`, default Sonnet 5.5 through Bedrock, no sampling parameters, 60-second deadline, tracked in model health like the others, usage in the `[llm]` log line) with the last six turns for follow-ups; retrieval uses the current question only. The model answers in JSON (`answer` with `[n]` markers, and `used`, the numbers it relied on).
- **Citations are built by the server** from the retrieved results the model says it used, never from model text: a number that matches no result is dropped, and each citation carries source, title, date, link, glob and status; a replaced choice also carries the newer choice's title and date, which the panel shows as "replaced by ..." and links when it can. When nothing is retrieved, when the model uses no source or its answer isn't usable JSON, the reply is "I couldn't find anything about that in the board's records." with no citations; with nothing retrieved and no board state the model isn't called.
- **Storage and routes:** `board_chat_messages` (migration 0029), one conversation per person per board (a member never sees another's). `GET /api/boards/:b/chat` returns the last 50 messages, `POST` takes `question` (at most 2,000 characters), optional `history`, `glob`, `group` and returns the question and reply messages, `DELETE` clears the conversation. A busy or unavailable model answers 503 `llm_unavailable` with the reason and fix, and nothing is stored, so the person asks again; the panel tells busy ("Bedrock is busy") from down. Members only.
- **MCP:** `ask_board(board, question, globId?)` returns the answer and its citations without reading or writing the person's conversation.
- **Better retrieval (s15t40):** before searching, one Haiku call (the search model, 10-second deadline, usage in the `[llm]` log line) rewrites the question and the last turns into a search query (typos fixed, "that" and "it" resolved); search uses the rewrite, the answering model still sees the person's own words, and any rewrite failure searches the question as typed. Every question also carries one extra numbered source, "Board state (now)", built from the store: open globs (not signed off or merged, most recently updated first, with type, status, list, waiting-for, run state, failure and head checks) and the board's active notifications, cut at about 4,000 characters; it is live state, not a record of decisions, and its citation links to the board. It is omitted when the board has neither, and the model is called when either it or a search hit exists. A reply that isn't usable JSON, or uses no source without saying it doesn't know, is logged as a `[chat]` warning (board, hit count, first 200 characters of the reply) and still shows "I couldn't find anything".
- **Chat upgrade (s15f26):** the answer is Markdown with `[n]` markers (no longer JSON); the server reads the cited numbers from the text, so citations still come from what was retrieved. `POST /api/boards/:b/chat` streams over server-sent events when the client sends `Accept: text/event-stream` (`text` pieces, then `done` with the stored messages or `error` with the status and body), through the optional `Llm.stream` port (Bedrock `ConverseStream`; needs `bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream`). The Stop button closes the request, which ends the model call and stores nothing. Conversations are `board_chats` (migration 0035, which also moves each person's earlier messages into their first conversation, titled by its first question) with `board_chat_messages.chat_id`: `GET /api/boards/:b/chats`, `GET` and `DELETE /api/boards/:b/chats/:c`; a question without `chat` starts a new conversation (*New chat*). Each page declares what it shows (`usePageContext`); the panel's scope chip follows it, can be pinned or widened to the whole board, and a glob page limits search to that glob and tells the model which page it is. Suggested questions and the actions under an answer adapt to the page. Each answer lists the steps it took (searches, live state, stronger model), chips are coloured by source type, glob IDs in an answer get compact cards, and *Include history* turns itself on for questions about the past (`impliesHistory`). *Think harder* answers one question with `CHAT_THINK_MODEL` (default Opus 5.5, 120-second deadline). Actions: *Create glob* (opens the form with the answer as its request text), *Attach to cited globs* (an open inbox item), *Save to knowledge* / *Propose a change* (`POST /api/boards/:b/chats/:c/messages/:m/save`: the answer goes to the proposal queue as a `pattern` learning whose source glob is the first one it cites, else the page's; it is never indexed on its own) and *Open in glob view*.
- **Chat manners (s15t52):** a greeting, thanks or a question about the chat itself (`isSmallTalk`, or the rewrite call's `smallTalk` flag, so no extra call) gets a short friendly reply from one model call with no search, sources or actions. The answering model may end an answer with one `<actions>create_glob, save</actions>` line (stripped from the stream and parsed by `parseActions`); the server keeps it on the message (`board_chat_messages.actions`, migration 0036) and drops it when the answer cites nothing, says it couldn't find anything, or is a miss (a miss carries its own buttons: Think harder, Look at the code, Create a glob; an uncited best-effort answer is kept and shown as "Not from the board's records"). The panel shows *Create glob* only when the model named it, *Save to knowledge* only when it named `save` and the answer cites a glob, *Open in glob view* only when the answer is about exactly one glob (or the page is one), and nothing under small talk or "couldn't find". No dividing lines between messages or above the input; the scope chip is hidden for the whole board and otherwise reads "About s15f26 · title" with pin and × (widen to the whole board); the panel never scrolls sideways (long lines wrap, code blocks scroll inside themselves).
- **Chat reads the code (s15f59):** on a board with a repository, `ChatService` (given the `CodeViewService`) lets the answering model look at the code before it answers, through a capped loop in the text protocol the size forecast and the reviewer already use (the `Llm` port is unchanged: `complete` and `stream` return text). The system prompt describes five calls, and a reply that starts with `{` and has a `"tool"` is a look rather than an answer (`parseCodeCall`): `tree` (a directory), `grep` (a pattern, optionally under one path), `read` (a file, optionally a line range), `history` (the commits that changed a path) and `imports` (what a file imports or is imported by). Each runs through `CodeViewService` on the base branch only (`ref` null, read-only, membership-checked, results capped) and is counted as consumer `chat`; its result is fed back with the earlier ones and the model is called again. The loop ends on an answer, or after 6 calls (`CHAT_CODE_MAX_CALLS`, the size forecast's budget): the next turn is told it may not look any more, and a model that still asks gets "I couldn't find anything". A request that can't be made (a path outside the repository, a bad pattern) is told to the model and counts against the cap. The deadline (60 s, 120 s for *Think harder*) covers the whole loop, not each call, and the person's Stop ends it; an expired deadline is the usual 503 `llm_unavailable`. Only the final answer streams: a turn that opens with `{` is held back (`withoutCodeRequests`). Every call shows in the answer's steps ("Read apps/server/src/main.ts:40-80", "Searched the code for retry: 3 hits", "Listed apps/server: 12 entries", "Looked up the history of ...", "Looked up what imports ..."). Each file the model reads becomes a numbered source after the records and the board state, shown to it as `[n] path:start-end`; citing it makes a citation with source `code` (label `Code`, title `path:start-end`, no link, a teal chip), and a number the model invents is dropped as for records. Grep, tree, history and imports results are for finding the way and are not citable. A question the records already answer needs no look (the model chooses), and the loop is also offered when the records and the board state have nothing, so a pure code question can be answered. *Fallback:* with no code view, a board without a repository, or a code view that can't read it, chat is as before; the system prompt then tells the model that if the question needs the code and it can't read it, it replies with exactly "I couldn't read the board's code to answer that." (`CHAT_NO_CODE`), which is stored as the answer with no sources and the step "Couldn't read the code". A call that fails mid-loop is reported to the model as unreadable, so it can say the same. *Not done:* branch refs other than the base branch, writing code or opening PRs from chat, and citations for grep hits.
- **Not built:** the agentic tool loop (Claude choosing searches and `get_glob`), summarising older turns, reranking, and prompt caching or a per-question token cap beyond the retrieval budget.
- **Slop help and misses (s15f137):** slop's own documentation is a search source, `slop_help` (label "Slop help"), indexed on every board by the same index sweep from files read at startup: the behaviour spec (one item per `##` section), the feature guide `docs/guide.md`, and the agent catalog's README, CLAUDE.md template and commands; nothing else in the repository. Its items rank in the discussion tier, below the board's own records, and chat also runs a second search over help alone so a how-to question always has help sources. A reply with text but no valid citation is kept and shown as "Not from the board's records" (stored with `uncited`; no buttons, never saved to knowledge). A true miss (the model has nothing to say, or there was nothing to search) replaces the bare sentence with one naming the rewritten query, how many records and help sections were found and whether the code was read, with buttons Think harder (once, when a stronger model exists), Look at the code (on a board with a repository) and Create a glob; the buttons are decided by the server, never by the model (stored with `miss`).

**As built (slice 8, choices and replacement; the code and storage still say decisions and supersession)**

- **Where choices come from:** the text under `## Decisions` of each glob's implementation record (or legacy postplan), the whole of plan.md, and attachments labelled Clarifications or Assumptions (the latest version of each). Attached inbox items (see the inbox notes below) are read as sources too; Slack has no producer yet. Approved learnings are no longer recorded as choices (the `decision` learning type is removed); rows made that way before stay as history until their source is gone.
- **Tables:** migration `0027_decisions` adds `decisions` (one row per choice, name kept, with the knowledge item it is searched by, glob and group, statement, verbatim quote, who and when, source kind, ref, label and link, and its supersession: `replaced_by`, `replace_state`, the two quotes and a reason, `checked_at` and the check's retry state) and `decision_sources` (per board and source ref: the content hash last extracted, `pending`, `done` or `failed`, attempts, `process_after`, last error). A choice's knowledge item has source type `decision` (kept), authority from its source (`choiceAuthority`: 0.95 record, 0.9 plan or attachment, 0.6 inbox) and external ref `decision:<source ref>:<12 hex of a hash of its normalised quote>`, so an edited source keeps the choices whose quote is still in it. Deleting a glob deletes its choices and releases older choices they replaced.
- **Extraction:** `DecisionPipeline.syncBoard` (the class that extracts choices) (run for a board about 10 seconds after a change, at start and every 15 minutes, like the index) hashes each source and queues the new or changed ones; `processNext` extracts one per call with one Haiku call (the search model, `SEARCH_MODEL`; job `decisions`, paused with that model). The answer is a strict JSON list of statement, quote, who and date. A decision is kept only when its quote is found word for word in the text shown to the model (case, whitespace and sentence punctuation at the ends ignored) and is at least four words and 20 characters; no quote means no decision. At most 40 decisions per source and 30,000 characters per source are read. The date is the model's only when it parses as an ISO date, else the artifact's date. Re-running changes nothing; a changed source replaces its decisions and keeps the status and replacing decision of those still quoted; a decision whose quote left the source is deleted. While the model is unavailable a source waits (a minute, or a growing wait when Bedrock is busy) without spending attempts; other failures retry with backoff and fail after three attempts.
- **Replacement (stored as supersession):** each new choice is checked once, after the sources are extracted. Candidates are active, earlier (not later than the new one) decisions on the board that are near it by keyword search or, when the embedder works, by meaning (similarity 0.75 or more), then those on the same glob or group, at most six. One Haiku call names the candidates the new decision contradicts or replaces, each with a quote from the old and the new decision. A replacement is applied (the old decision's item becomes `superseded`, linked to the new item) only when both quotes are found verbatim in the decisions' own text and are long enough, the old decision is still active and the new one is not itself superseded (so no cycle); anything else (the quote fails, the model doesn't class the subject as the same, or the older choice is in another glob that the newer one doesn't name) is dropped: nothing is stored and no decision glob is raised (see **Replacement cards**). A pair a person undid is never applied again. States: `applied`, `confirmed`, `undone` (`hint` is retired). Before the model is asked, an earlier choice the new one restates (the same words once case, punctuation and spacing are ignored; one contains the other and is at least 80% of it; or word overlap of 0.9 or more) is replaced at once with reason "same choice restated", on any glob, with no model call and no naming requirement. An embedder failure only drops the semantic candidates; a model that is unavailable makes the check wait without spending attempts, and three failures count the decision as checked.
- **Replacement cards (retired, s15t93):** choices are a buffer that takes input without putting decision globs in front of a person, so a replacement slop can't verify is dropped, not asked about (s15f66's cards raised 37 in one second on 2026-10-12, about half of them false positives). A replacement is applied only when it is checked and allowed (above) or a restatement; otherwise nothing is written. The decision sweep (`syncBoard`) retires what the old pipeline left: every open card with origin `replacement` is closed (Signed off, archived, outcome `closed`, reason "Replacement cards retired") and every `hint` row is cleared, so the older choice stands. Closed cards still render as plain decision globs, and Confirm, Dismiss and Undo still work on an old `applied` or `confirmed` replacement. The number of dropped replacements by reason is not yet counted in the context report.
- **Person control:** `GET /api/boards/:b/globs/:g/decisions` lists the choices made on a glob (members), each with its source link and what replaced it and what it replaced; `POST /api/boards/:b/decisions/:id/confirm` turns a hint into `confirmed` and supersedes the older decision; `POST .../undo` turns an applied, confirmed or proposed replacement into `undone` and the older decision is active again. The glob view shows them under Choices: a replaced one struck through with "Replaced by ... on <date>" (with Undo), a hint as "Proposed: replaced by ..." with a link to its replacement card; the Confirm and Dismiss of the older choice's own view are used only for a hint that has no card yet. A `glob.decisions` hint refreshes the list.
- **Search and context:** `decision` is a source type (kept for compatibility; label "Choice"). In current mode a current choice ranks above every replaced choice, and replaced results are labelled `replaced by "<title>" on <date>` (with `supersededBy: {title, date}` on the hit, name kept); all-time mode shows both with the labels and no penalty. `related` in `get_context` puts up to three current choices on the topic first, only those of authority 0.9 or more (records and plans; choices from discussion compete as ordinary results), then the other hits (replaced choices labelled), at most five in all. The bundle also has `decisions` (the glob's own choices, name kept, current first, each with its source and what replaced it) and `authority` (the order of authority above). The search indexer never removes decision items.
- **Usage and raising (s15f67):** choices are a buffer: the few that matter reach the board and the rest fade. Each return of a choice to an agent (`get_context` related, `search_text`, `search_semantic`) or in a chat citation is counted per board, choice, day and glob in `choice_usage` (migration `0054_choice_usage`; a counter, no model call), and the weekly mining signal `choice_in_use:<choice id>` raises a `pattern` learning for one that keeps being returned and no document covers (see Signals slop mined). `GET /api/boards/:b/globs/:g/decisions` adds `usage: {returns, globs}` (last 30 days) to each choice, which the Choices list shows as "used N times on M globs".
- **Not yet:** choices from Slack, marking a choice wrong, re-checking replacement for an old choice when a newer one is edited, and comparing a choice with earlier-dated choices that arrive after its one check.

**As built (slice 9, inbox and paste)**

- **Scope:** the board inbox and paste only (Slack is added below). Meetings from Apps Script and the other adapters, the cross-board shared inbox, and *create glob from an item* are later parts of slice 9. Routes are board-scoped (`/api/boards/:b/inbox...`), not the global `/inbox` of the interface table: every other store and route is per board, and paste is the only producer so far. Attaching is to globs on the item's own board.
- **Tables:** migration `0029_inbox` adds `inbox_items` (the pasted text, optional title, source `paste` and its label, source type, `occurred_at`, a hash of the whitespace-normalised text that is unique per board, status `new | attached | kept | archived | discarded`, the summary, the suggestions as JSON `[{globId, reason}]`, the summary step's state `pending | done | failed` with attempts, `process_after` and `last_error`, the search item it is indexed as, and a version for conditional writes) and `inbox_links` (item and glob, with the attachment it put on the glob; no foreign key to the glob, so deleting a glob removes its links and an item whose last link went becomes `kept`).
- **Pasting:** the *Paste into inbox* dialog (text, optional title, date and source label) and the `add_to_inbox` MCP tool call `InboxService.add`, the one write path (`POST /api/boards/:b/inbox`, 201 `{id, created}`). One transaction stores the row and its knowledge item with chunks, so the text is keyword-searchable at once, even with Bedrock down; the index job embeds the chunks later. Pasting the same text again returns the existing item (`created: false`; text that was discarded comes back as new). Text is limited to 100,000 characters. The item is a `meeting` at paste; the summary step may reclassify it as `thread` or `doc`. All three source types carry the `discussion` authority tier (0.6), below plans and decisions, and are labelled Meeting, Thread and Document in citations. The search sweep (`SearchIndexer.syncBoard`) and glob deletion leave them alone: `InboxService` and `InboxPipeline` own them, and a glob's deletion only removes the glob from the item's links.
- **Summary and suggestions:** `InboxPipeline.processNext` (job `inbox`, paused with `SEARCH_MODEL`) takes the oldest pending item under a two-minute lease and makes one Haiku call, with the text (cut at 30,000 characters) and up to eight candidate globs (open ones found by the title's keywords when a title was given, and by meaning from the title and the start of the text when the embedder works; each with its summary and best matching text). The answer is strict JSON: a title (used only when none was pasted), the kind, a summary of at most 80 words, and up to three suggestions, each a candidate glob with a one-sentence reason; a glob that was not a candidate is dropped. A changed title or kind re-chunks the item once; a finished item is never asked again. While Bedrock is busy or unavailable the item waits with the usual messages and no attempt is spent (growing waits for busy, a minute otherwise); other failures retry with backoff and mark the item failed after three attempts, and it stays listed and searchable without a summary. The pasted text is untrusted: the prompt says so, and the UI shows it as plain text.
- **Inbox page:** an *Inbox* tab beside *Board* (count of new items) lists items newest first with title, source, date, summary (or the start of the text and what it waits for), suggested globs with their reasons and an Attach button each, and the globs it is on. *Attach to...* picks the suggestions and any other open glob; *Keep* and *Discard* settle it. `?item=<id>` (the target of the glob's attachment link) highlights one. Hints (`board.inbox`) refresh the page, and it polls every 15 seconds while an item is being summarised.
- **Attach, keep, discard:** `POST /api/boards/:b/inbox/:id/attach` (`{globIds}`, 1 to 10 globs, all on the board) writes, for each glob not yet linked and in one transaction, a link attachment labelled "From the inbox: <title>" (with the item's number added when another item with the same title is on the glob) through the artifact write path, records the link, and sets the item `attached` and the globs on its search item (no re-chunking). Attaching again changes nothing. `keep` moves a new item to `kept` (still indexed); `discard` removes the item and its chunks from search and is refused while the item is attached. `POST .../:id/detach` (`{globIds}`) undoes an attach for each glob the item is on, in one transaction: it deletes the link, appends an empty version of the glob's link attachment (history kept; an attachment whose latest version has no text and no link is left out of artifact listings), sets the globs on the search item, and drops the decision source `inbox:<item>:<glob>` with its decisions (older decisions they replaced stand again). An item left on no glob becomes `kept`, so it can then be discarded. A glob the item is not on changes nothing. The Inbox tab and the glob view's inbox attachment both offer Detach, after a confirmation. `GET /api/boards/:b/inbox[?status=]` lists (default: new, attached and kept) and `GET .../:id` returns one with its text. All of them are for board members.
- **Context and decisions:** `get_context` gains `inbox`: the items attached to the glob with their summary and text up to 12,000 characters (`truncated` beyond; `search_text` finds the rest). Each attached item is also a choice source on every glob it is on (`inbox:<id>:<glob>`, source kind `inbox`, a choice without a date takes the item's date), so the glob view shows the choices with a link to the item.

**As built (slice 9, part 2: Meet notes through an Apps Script)**

- **Integration ingest:** `POST /integrations/boards/:b/inbox` (outside `/api`, which needs a person) takes `{source: 'meet', sourceRef, text, title?, occurredAt?, sourceLabel?}` with the board's integration token as a bearer credential, and calls `InboxService.deliver`. 201 `{id, created: true}`, or 200 `{id, created: false}` for a source ref already delivered (even one that was discarded or whose doc was edited since: the first delivery stands). 401 for a missing, wrong, revoked or other-board's token (all alike), 422 for a bad body, 413 over 400 KB. The token can do nothing but add inbox items to its own board.
- **Dedupe:** migration `0031_integration_tokens` adds `inbox_items.source_ref` (empty for a paste) with a unique index on `(board, source, source_ref)` where the ref is set; the Meet doc's ID is the ref. The existing per-board content-hash uniqueness still applies, so two docs with identical text are one item.
- **Integration token:** table `integration_tokens` keeps only the SHA-256 of the secret (`slopit_` plus 32 random bytes), one active per board (partial unique index). Board admins create (the secret is in that response only, `cache-control: no-store`), replace (the old is revoked) and revoke it with `POST` and `DELETE /api/boards/:b/integration-token`; any member can `GET` whether one is active. The board's Settings page has an *Integration token* section for it, under Members and access.
- **The script:** `integrations/meet-notes/` (`Code.gs`, `appsscript.json`, `README.md`) runs from a 10-minute time trigger, finds Google docs modified since its last run (kept in Script Properties, with a five-minute overlap) that are titled "... Notes by Gemini" or sit in a "Meet Recordings" folder, exports each as plain text and posts it with the doc's ID as the source reference, the meeting title and day from the doc's title, and the doc's URL at the end of the text. A failed delivery stops the run and the next one resumes from before it. Its pure selection functions are unit-tested by loading `Code.gs` in a VM.

**As built (slice 9, part 3: the Slack app)**

- **Slack (slice 9, part 3):** the app in `integrations/slack/manifest.yml` has a message shortcut, *Send to slop* (`callback_id` `send_to_slop`), the scopes `commands`, `channels:history`, `groups:history` and `users:read`, and the interactivity URL `POST /integrations/slack/interactivity`. The route is on only when the server has `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET` and `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`; `SLACK_WORKSPACES` (`<workspace id>=<board number>,...`) maps each workspace to its board, so one bot token serves one workspace for now. It verifies Slack's `v0` signature over the raw body and refuses a timestamp more than five minutes off (401), acks with an empty 200 at once, and in the background reads the thread (`conversations.replies`, from the thread root or the message itself), looks up names (`users.info`) and the root's permalink (`chat.getPermalink`), and calls `InboxService.addFromSource`: source `slack`, source type `thread`, the permalink as `source_ref`, the thread as text with a name and UTC time on each message. It uses `inbox_items.source_ref` (from `0031_integration_tokens`, unique with board and source when set): sending the same thread again updates the item (new text, summary and suggestions again, links kept) instead of adding one. The sender gets an ephemeral reply (via the payload's `response_url`) with a link to the inbox item, or the error: workspace not linked, the app not in the channel, or Slack's error code. No member check applies (Slack's signature and the workspace mapping are the authority); `created_by` is `slack:<user id>`.

**As built (slice 9, part 4: Jira and Google Docs backfill)**

- **Backfill (slice 9, part 4):** `slop import jira --board <n> --project <KEY> [--jql ...] [--since <date>] [--docs]` reads a Jira Cloud project in the CLI (the site, email and API token come from `JIRA_SITE_URL`, `JIRA_EMAIL` and `JIRA_API_TOKEN`; with `--docs` a Google bearer token from `GOOGLE_ACCESS_TOKEN`; nothing is stored on the server) and delivers batches of up to 50 to `POST /api/boards/:b/inbox/import` (`InboxService.importItems`). Each issue becomes one item with source `jira`, type `thread` and its key as `source_ref` (from `0031_integration_tokens`; unique per board and source); each Google Doc linked in an issue's text or web links becomes a `gdoc` item of type `doc`, keyed by its document id. Imported items are `archived` (indexed for text and meaning search and attachable, but out of the default inbox list; `?status=archived` lists them) and start already summarised, so a backfill spends no model calls. A re-run skips an unchanged item (same key and content hash), updates a changed one in place and re-indexes it, and leaves one a person discarded alone; text over 100,000 characters is cut. The command prints counts of added, updated, skipped and failed, and lists Docs it could not fetch without failing on them. Ongoing sync, attachments and mapping issues to globs are not built.

- **Create glob from an item (s15f70):** each item (new, kept, attached or archived, so Jira imports too; not discarded) has *Create glob* on the Inbox page. It opens the create-glob dialog with the request set to the item's title, summary and text (cut to 19,000 characters, which intake takes) and the note "From the inbox: <title>. The item will be attached."; everything stays editable. `POST /api/boards/:b/globs` and MCP `create_glob` take an optional `inboxItem` (an item id of the same board): inside the create's own transaction, after the glob is written, `linkInboxItem` (the code `attach` uses) adds the link attachment and sets the item `attached` (an archived one too), so plan.md's context, `get_context` and decisions include it from the start. The item is checked before anything is written: another board's or an unknown item is `not_found`, a discarded one `invalid_input`, and a racing change to the item rolls the glob back too (`invalid_input`, nothing made). The board creates with the key `inbox:<id>`, so a repeat finds the first glob (`GET /api/boards/:b/inbox/:id/glob` returns its id or null) and the card offers to open it or *Create another* (a fresh key). Callers of `create_glob` pass their own idempotency key.\n- **Not yet:** detach and a re-run of a failed summary.

**Context tuning (s15f68)**

The numbers that decide what search, choices and the inbox hand to agents are per-board settings, not constants, and a weekly job moves the ones that can be learned from what people did with the results.

- **Settings:** thirteen keys, each with a default (today's value, written once in the core and read from there by the code that used the old constants), hard bounds and a step. A board's stored value is clamped to the bounds on every read, and a board with nothing stored runs on the defaults (`context_settings`, one row per key that differs). *Learned* (the job may move them): discussion weight (0.6, 0.3 to 0.95, step 0.05: how much pasted discussion counts against records and plans in ranking), recency half-life (90 days, 30 to 365, step 15), replacement similarity (0.75, 0.6 to 0.9, step 0.025: how close two choices must be before a replacement is proposed), inbox candidates (8, 4 to 16, step 1: the globs the inbox offers the model), and the returns a choice needs before it is raised as a learning: 5 from a record or plan (3 to 10) and 10 from discussion (5 to 20), step 1. *Fixed* (shown, never moved: min, max and default are equal): the replaced-choice factor (0.15), related choices (3) and related results (5) in `get_context`, the choices extracted per source (40), the source text read (30,000 characters), inbox suggestions (3) and the globs a choice must be returned on (3).
- **Signals:** only counts and outcomes already stored, no model calls: choices (`replaceState`, source kind, created), promoted learnings (mined decision globs for `choice_in_use` and their outcome), replacement cards (their outcome), retrievals of choices older than the half-life and whether the glob's implementation record names them, and inbox attaches against the suggestions. Retrievals are also counted per day by source type and ranking tier (`context_retrievals`, written when search, related results or chat return hits; pruned after 90 days like `choice_usage`).
- **Job:** `context_tuning`, weekly (Run now in the Context tuning panel of "What slop has learned"; a board job like mining, under the same lease). Each learned setting gets one rule and moves at most one step a run, clamped to its bounds, and holds when the evidence is short (a rule needs 5 to 10 decided items depending on the setting). A setting changed (moved or reset) in the last 7 days holds, and only evidence dated after its last change counts, so one finding cannot move it again the next week. Rules, in brief: discussion weight goes down when choices from discussion are replaced or dismissed more than twice as often as record and plan choices, and up when promoted discussion and plan choices are accepted at about the same rate (down wins); the promotion minimums rise when more than 60% of raised learnings are rejected and fall when more than 90% are accepted; replacement similarity falls when over 60% of proposed replacements are confirmed and rises when over 60% are dismissed; the half-life lengthens when old choices are cited in at least 40% of their retrievals and shortens when at most 10% are; inbox candidates rise when over 60% of attaches go to a glob that was not suggested.
- **History:** every move and every reset is a row in `context_setting_changes` (when, the value before and after, an outcome word and the evidence in plain words), newest first in the panel's collapsed history. Nothing moves silently.
- **Reset:** admins put one setting back on its default from the Context tuning panel (`POST /api/boards/{b}/context-settings/{key}/reset`); the reset is a history row with the outcome `reset` and counts as a change for the 7-day rule. There is no manual edit: a person who disagrees with a value resets it, and the job may move it again only on new evidence.
- **Report:** `GET /api/boards/{b}/context-report` (members) returns, for the last 30 days: the share of retrievals by source type and by tier; the 20 most retrieved choices with the globs behind them; choices by source, new this week, replaced (applied automatically, confirmed, undone) and the cards and promoted learnings people decided; the inbox's attaches to a suggested glob against another glob, and the items kept and discarded; every setting with its value, default, bounds, learned flag and effect; and the history. The Context tuning panel shows it, with the `context_tuning` job beneath it. Choices that contradict a knowledge document are not listed: the contradiction checks run on open learnings and their result is not stored against a choice, so the report says so instead of guessing. Known limits: retrievals of knowledge items other than choices are counted by source and tier but not per item, so the half-life rule reads choices only; a retrieval with no glob (a search or chat question) says nothing about citation and is left out of that rule.

## Decision globs and the knowledge page

A decision glob has no slop type: its `type` is null (existing decision globs, which were stored as `same`, were migrated to null).

A **decision glob** (category `decision`, ID `s<board>k<n>` from the board's `k` counter) is the board's record of a proposed change to what the project knows or decides. A **KB item is its decision glob**: a learning an agent submits (`submit_learning`) or slop mines (mining, effect checks, escalation outcomes) is created as a decision glob in Planning under its own `k` ID, and every KB read and write goes through that one record: reads use the typed helpers in `domain/learning.ts` (and `learningWire` for the wire form agents and the Knowledge page receive), writes use the named glob operations in `app/learning-writes.ts`. There is no separate KB-item type or adapter. Inbox candidates and extracted choices a person promotes are decision globs too, with no learning behind them (a promoted choice is then something a person decides, not a choice any more). A decision is something a person decides, never a thing slop extracts (those are choices, see Choices and replacement), and it is not work: it never has a branch, PR, routine run, time report, size check, intake snapshot or merge-policy hold, and it has no type (`null`).

**Data.** The glob's jsonb document carries `attributes` (`{}` reserved for work): `scope`, `origin` (`submitted` or `mined` for a learning, `inbox`, `extracted`, `replacement` with the `oldId` and `newId` of the two choices, `manual`), `channel`, `outcome`, `conflictsWith` and `archived`, and for a learning its own fields: `kind` (`gotcha`, `pattern` or `agent-behaviour`; the old `decision` kind is removed and a migration moves existing decision globs of kind `decision` to `pattern`), `sourceGlobs`, `target` (`suggested`, `routed`), `agentSetVersion`, `signal`, `evidenceCount`, `catalogCandidate`, `catalogReason`, `closedAs`, `stale`, `document` (a whole-document proposal's name and frontmatter), and the text references that name no glob (`coveredByText`, `possiblyCoveredByText`, `contradictsText`). How learnings relate is a separate list, `links` (`duplicate_of`, `suppressed_by`, `covered_by`, `possibly_covered_by` with the hint's quotes, `contradicts` with its note, `kept_apart_from`; source `pipeline` or `explicit`), kept apart from the dependency edges (`after`, `impliedAfter`) so it never holds a start or orders a merge; `get_glob` returns it as `links`. The payload is the `plan` artifact (a learning's statement and `## Evidence`, or a proposed document's body), so `get_plan`, `save_plan` and the plan editor work unchanged. Special artifacts hold the `draft` (its section, the target and target version it was drafted against, and why, in provenance), the approval's `outcome` and the `effect_check`, each a new version when it changes and empty when cleared; attachments hold evidence near-duplicates added (`evidence:<item>`) and the `merge_note`. `assessment` is a special artifact: listed, never inlined. Validation in core rejects unknown attribute keys; only `scope` is client-editable (admins and POs, `PATCH /api/globs/:id`). A learning's version is the glob's version: any write to the glob (a pick-up, a scope edit) is a new version the Knowledge page's next write must match. Its pipeline state (`processing`, error, attempts, next try) is read from its step's newest outbox job; a decided or closed learning reads as out of the pipeline (`processing: none`).

**States.** Statuses reuse the lists: Planning, Doing (`in_progress`), Reviewing, Signed Off. Every status change is a row of the machine.

| From | Who and what | To |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Planning | admin or PO picks up (no provisioning, no run) | Doing |
| Doing | admin or PO takes over | Doing |
| Doing | Accept, Accept with edits (the learning is approved in the same write: its change applied, the outcome `accept` or `accept_with_edits`) | Reviewing |
| any open state | the learning is approved on the Knowledge page or by an agent | Reviewing |
| Doing | Retarget (the learning is drafted again against the new target) | Doing |
| Doing | Needs work (a work glob is linked, or created as a task; the decision waits) | Doing, then Reviewing when that glob merges (`release_waiting`) |
| Doing, or any open state from the Knowledge page | Reject with a reason (the learning is rejected) | Signed Off, archived |

Accepting (with or without edits), rejecting, the pipeline closing the item and signing a decision off each queue the `release_waiting` effect: the globs with a relation `until: decided` (accepted, rejected or closed; needs work and retarget are not decided) or `until: signed_off` to the decision are released by the same path as after a merge (row 33, and rows 14b and 21b for a held merge or sign-off). A decision is never the glob held back: a relation whose `from` is a decision is refused.
| Reviewing | admin or PO signs off (refused while a `sign_off` relation of the decision is unmet) | Signed Off |
| Reviewing | admin or PO reopens (outcome cleared, to choose again; an approved learning stays approved, and accepting again only records the outcome) | Doing |
| any open state | the KB pipeline closes the learning (merged into another, suppressed, covered) | Signed Off, archived, outcome `closed` by slop, `closedAs` and the link to what closed it (none for a document's text) |
| Signed Off, closed by the pipeline | an admin reopens it (glob view or Knowledge page; `closedAs`, `archived`, the outcome, the implementer and the closing link go; an undone merge becomes a `kept_apart_from` link on both) | Planning |
| Reviewing or Signed Off, decided by an agent | an admin reopens the agent's decision (its change reverted) | Planning |

**Outcomes** go through `POST /api/globs/:id/decision` (`outcome`, `reason`, `editedDraft`, `target`, `linkedGlobId`, `version`). Admins and POs may decide; a PO may not decide a learning that changes an agent-set file, the local-run spec or the merge policy (an admin does), except to reject it. For a learning, accept and reject are the learning's own approval or rejection, written once: the decision row only checks the move may happen. A decision with no learning (an inbox or extracted candidate) records the outcome only; Accept with edits and Retarget are not offered for it. Work commands (start, retrigger, merge, labels and the rest) are refused on a decision, and decision commands on a work glob.

**Deleting.** A learning (origin `submitted` or `mined`) is never deleted: it is rejected instead, and a rejected learning suppresses its repeats. The glob view offers no Delete for one, and the server refuses it. Admins and POs may delete a decision a person made (inbox, extracted, manual).

**Scope** says what a decision governs: `glob` (one glob), `future_work` (globs created after it, optionally in one group), `area_group` (a group; risk-area names are deferred) or `project`. Defaults: `project` for a learning migrated from kind `decision` (now `pattern`), otherwise `future_work`. Only accepted decisions apply (Reviewing or Signed Off with an accept outcome, not archived). They reach work two ways: a new glob (and each new part of a split) gets one `Decisions` attachment listing the governing decisions (IDs, scope and a one-line statement, not inline), and `get_context` computes `scopedDecisions` live, so a decision accepted after the glob was created still reaches it. When a learning contradicts another decision with a broader scope, the narrower glob shows a "Conflicts with" chip (`conflictsWith`; structural, no model), recomputed when its contradictions change and when the broader one is accepted.

**Channels:** `agent` (`submit_learning`, which returns the item's ID as `globId`), `mined` (mining, effect checks and escalation outcome learning), `inbox` (`POST /api/boards/:b/inbox/:id/promote`, which also attaches the item as the source) and `human` (an extracted choice, `POST /api/boards/:b/decisions/:id/promote`; the path keeps the old name). `create_glob` and the REST create refuse category `decision`; intake never proposes it.

**Assessment.** A deterministic markdown template (no model): what is proposed, scope, target, diff size, evidence count, flags (contradicts, possibly covered, catalog candidate, stale draft, scope conflicts) and a suggested outcome by fixed rules (covered: reject; contradicts: needs work; drafted with no flags: accept). The latest one is never older than the draft and never says there is no suggested outcome: a rules recommendation (`RULES_MODEL`, "Leaning: Approve, waiting for the draft. …" before a draft exists) is written when a learning is created, routed, drafted or re-drafted and when its conflict flags move, and never replaces a model answer for the current draft; for people's channels on demand (`POST /api/globs/:id/assessment`, the Assess button). It shows beside the review section of the glob view. The assessment stays rule-based; the model's recommendation is **Suggest**, below.

**Suggest (s15f74).** On a decision glob, admins and POs press Suggest (the Assess button's new name; `POST /api/globs/:id/suggest`, MCP `suggest_decision`; never automatic: the automatic recommendation is the rules one above). It queues a `suggest_decision` outbox job on the `llm` lane (one unclaimed job per glob). The job makes one call to `SUGGEST_MODEL` (Sonnet, global inference profile; spend source `suggest`) that reads the learning (statement, type, evidence, source globs, occurrences), its draft and the diff it would make, the target section's current text and the document's headings, the other open learnings on the same file with their drafts (at most 8), work globs merged since the learning was submitted whose title or summary overlaps its area (at most 8, titles and summaries only), the last 5 outcomes on the same file with their reasons, and the rule-based flags; every part is cut to a bounded size. The answer is JSON: `action` (`approve`, `approve_with_edit`, `merge_into`, `reject`), `reason`, and `editedDraft` / `mergeInto` + `mergedText` where the action needs them. Core checks it against the input: an unknown `mergeInto`, a missing text, or a text-needing action on a decision with no knowledge item (those get approve or reject only) throws, which spends an attempt of the job; a model that is down or busy waits without one. It is stored as a new version of the `assessment` artifact labelled `suggestion` (Markdown, then the structured fields in a fenced block) with the model and a stamp of the draft artifact's version and the target's version; `GET /api/globs/:id/suggestion` marks it stale when either moved. Apply pre-fills the decide form (approve; approve with the edit; reject with the reason; a merge pre-fills this learning's rejection, "Merged into <id>: …", and shows the combined text and a link for the other learning's own approval). Nothing is decided without the person's confirmation.

**Diff.** `GET /api/globs/:id/diff` returns the learning's target, the target's current version, the `DraftPreview` (the same line diff the Knowledge page shows, computed by `KnowledgeService.previewFor`) and the draft text; the glob view shows it with the shared collapsible diff component and the Knowledge page uses the same one.

**Learning view.** A learning's outcome is the action row's main button under the title (the recommended outcome, else Approve change; "Waiting for the draft", disabled, while drafting), with Reject…, Approve with edits…, Approve without changing a document and Get a new recommendation in its ▾; the body has no deciding buttons. It shows Why, the change (headed "New section in" only when `sectionExisted` from `/diff` is false, else "Addition to", "Edit to" or "No change to"), Recommendation, Applies to, Where it came from ("From an agent", "From a person" or "The Matrix") and a collapsed Evidence and history; scope reads "This glob only", "All new globs", "New globs in group X" or "The whole project (existing and new globs)".

**Board and views.** Decision cards have an accent edge, a scope chip, the outcome and the conflict chip, no labels. The glob view's decision section shows a learning's kind, target, evidence count, how the pipeline closed it and its links (each opening the other decision). The Knowledge page's item IDs open their decisions in the glob view. The board's chips Work, Decisions and Mine start from the role (developers and QA: Work; POs: Decisions; admins: Work and Decisions) and are remembered per board in the browser. Archived decisions never show. New learnings reach open boards through the `board.kb` hint, which refetches the list.

**Migration.** Migration 0049 moved every KB item into its decision glob in one transaction with dropping the old table: open items in Planning; approvals of the last 30 days Signed Off, older ones archived (still read as approved, so `get_conventions` and search keep serving them); rejections archived with their reason; items the pipeline closed archived with `closedAs` and the closing link; their payload, draft, outcome, effect check, evidence and merge notes as artifacts and attachments, and created, status and outcome events dated as the originals. Extracted choices were not given globs: they stay candidates a person promotes. The pipeline's outbox jobs (migration 0048) kept pointing at the same IDs.

**Generated knowledge page.** Each board has a generated, versioned page (`GET /api/boards/:b/knowledge-page`, member read; admins queue a rebuild) with two sections: *Agents and skills*, read from the agent set's agent and command files, and *Key decisions*, the decisions in force grouped by area. Agent learnings (accepted learnings whose target is an agent-set file: kind agent, command, hook, settings, mcp or claude_md) are left out, since the agent set already holds them; project decisions (origins `inbox`, `extracted`, `manual`, and learnings that target knowledge documents) stay, at most the newest 150. Only the decisions section is written by a model, and only from what changed since the previous version: Haiku 4.5 (`KNOWLEDGE_PAGE_MODEL`, spend recorded as `knowledge_page`). Each entry carries `key` (true when a new developer or the product owner would need it to work on the project: a choice of architecture, tooling, process or product behaviour that is not obvious from the code; a missing `key` reads as false) and `at` (the newest decision time behind it, set by the service). The page shows key entries newest-backed first, at most 20; the rest are behind a collapsed "N more decisions", with a link to the board with the Decision category selected (the board's Signed Off column shows only the latest few, so it does not list every decision). A version written before the flag existed shows every entry. A regeneration runs at most once per board interval, by default 1440 minutes (a board setting, 5 to 1440; migration 0052 moved boards still at the old 60 to 1440), and writes nothing when nothing it is built from changed.
